Announcing .NET 11

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Today, we’re excited to announce the release of .NET 11! This year’s release makes building modern applications faster, more productive, and has improvements across C# 15, ASP.NET Core, .NET MAUI, and Windows development. With hundreds of runtime optimizations, more responsive SDK tooling, and less overhead in everyday commands, you can spend less time waiting and more time creating. Build richer web and cross-platform applications, take advantage of new C# language features, and explore AI-powered development with improved tools and agentic capabilities.

.NET 11 support .NET 11 is a Standard Term Support (STS) release with two years of support under the .NET support policy. STS and Long Term Support (LTS) releases have the same quality and servicing policies; the difference is the length of support. .NET 10 remains a supported LTS option. Stay current with servicing updates whichever release you choose.

Explore .NET 11 with the product teams and community at .NET Conf 2026. The conference includes product team sessions on November 10 and 11, followed by two days of community sessions.

Watch the Day 1 livestream, including the keynote, or browse the conference session playlist.

Some of the changes we’re most excited for you to try:

The what’s new in .NET 11 overview links to the full details. Here are the changes behind those headlines and how to start using them.

C# 15 is the default language version for projects targeting .NET 11. Union types are one of its biggest additions: a value can be one of a declared set of case types, with conversions from those types and exhaustiveness checking in switch expressions.

Consider a product lookup that either finds a product or reports that it wasn’t found:

The result type tells a caller which outcomes it needs to handle. There’s no need to use object or an unrelated base class just to put those outcomes behind one return type.

C# 15 also adds closed hierarchies. A closed class limits direct derivation to its declaring assembly, giving the compiler the information it needs to check patterns over the hierarchy. Unions are useful when you want to combine existing types; closed hierarchies fit models where you own the inheritance relationship.

Smaller language features help with everyday code, too. Collection expression arguments let you pass constructor or factory arguments without giving up collection expression syntax:

This prints 2: the comparer controls equality, so "Alice" and "ALICE" are one entry.

Other additions include extension indexers and labeled break and continue. These features don’t require the preview language version. The separate memory safety work remains in preview; see the C# 15 feature documentation for its opt-in requirements.

F# 11 improves compiler memory use and reduces allocations in FSharp.Core collection operations. It also adds ways to bound concurrent asynchronous work, useful when a collection operation would otherwise start more work than a service or machine can handle comfortably.

The F# release notes distinguish the default-language updates from preview capabilities, including Runtime Async support for custom computation-expression builders.

The SDK now enables a Native AOT CLI host by default. Commands such as help and version queries can run without starting the managed CLI, and tool launches can avoid that startup path too. Commands that need managed execution still use it.

The MSBuild server also stays warm between CLI invocations by default, reducing repeated startup work. SDK installers on Linux and macOS are smaller through assembly deduplication.

File-based apps can grow across source files with #:include, and solution filters can be created and edited from the CLI. dotnet watch adds Aspire app-host integration, crash recovery, and device selection for mobile projects.

For teams using Microsoft.Testing.Platform, dotnet test gains run-level timeouts, failure limits, clearer progress reporting, and per-module results directories. The test runner matters: these switches aren’t interchangeable with the VSTest command-line options.

Container publishing adds reproducible image digests through SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH and avoids redundant layer uploads when the target manifest already exists. NuGet source configuration gains a minimum package publication age setting, which lets teams wait before newly published package versions become eligible for selection. This is an additional policy control, not a replacement for vulnerability checks or reviewing dependencies.

See what’s new in the SDK and tooling for command examples and configuration.

Performance work in .NET 11 reaches both the code you deploy and the tools you use to develop it. Stephen Toub’s Performance Improvements in .NET 11 takes a detailed tour through hundreds of runtime and library optimizations, with benchmarks and code examples.

The just-in-time (JIT) compiler improvements remove more redundant bounds checks and branches, improve devirtualization, and fold more operations into constants. For example, the JIT can optimize additional SequenceEqual calls when it knows the inputs at compile time. These changes improve generated code without requiring you to rewrite the corresponding application logic.

Native AOT interface dispatch uses a shared helper that reduces the code needed at interface call sites and improves throughput for interface-heavy workloads. Hardware support also expands, including FP16 instructions for Half operations on supported x64 and Arm64 hardware and new vector construction and composition APIs.

There’s useful work on diagnostics, too. In-process crash report logging can capture a managed stack trace, module information, and runtime state before a process exits. Previously available on mobile platforms, this reporting path now also supports Linux and macOS.

As always, measure the effect on your own application. Throughput, startup time, memory use, and deployment size depend on the workload and publishing options you choose.

Runtime Async has been a recurring theme throughout the .NET 11 previews. It moves asynchronous suspension and resumption into the runtime instead of relying on compiler-generated state machines.

One especially useful result is a clearer live call stack. Profilers and debuggers can show the asynchronous methods themselves instead of the infrastructure used to execute them. This is a distinction from exception stack traces, which already hide much of that infrastructure.

Runtime Async remains a preview feature. To try it in a project targeting net11.0, add:

You don’t need to enable EnablePreviewFeatures for this scenario. The .NET runtime libraries themselves are built with Runtime Async, but opting your application’s code into the new compilation model is a separate choice. See the Runtime Async documentation for its behavior and current guidance, and test your application’s dependencies and diagnostics before adopting it.

The .NET 11 SDK includes an MCP server template. MCP gives AI applications a common way to discover and call tools and services. The template uses the official MCP C# SDK and includes a working sample tool, so you can start exposing your own functionality without first installing a separate template package:

For applications that consume AI services, Microsoft.Extensions.AI (MEAI) provides abstractions such as IChatClient and IEmbeddingGenerator. These libraries let you compose model access with dependency injection, caching, and OpenTelemetry using familiar .NET patterns. They’re available as NuGet packages, rather than features that require you to move all of your application code to a new framework version.

Microsoft Agent Framework (MAF) builds on these abstractions with agents that call tools and maintain conversation state, plus workflows that coordinate agents and application code. Connect agents to MCP tools to give them access to your application’s capabilities, and expose an agent through an ASP.NET Core AG-UI endpoint to build an interactive Blazor experience. The AG-UI hosting integration is currently prerelease.

For hands-on examples, start with .NET AI Essentials: The Core Building Blocks Explained and continue with our Microsoft Agent Framework walkthrough.

The .NET libraries add capabilities that previously required extra setup, intermediate buffers, or custom helpers.

The Process API expansion includes one-shot methods to run a process and capture its output and exit status. More advanced APIs give you control over inherited handles, suspended starts, and process signaling. Build tools and developer utilities can spend less code on process plumbing while still inspecting how a child process finished. See Process API Improvements in .NET 11 for examples.

Zstandard compression joins the existing compression formats in System.IO.Compression. New span-based compression APIs support callers that manage their own buffers, and ZIP improvements include CRC32 validation when reading entries.

Four new in-memory stream adapters bridge existing data to APIs that accept a Stream: ReadOnlyMemoryStream, WritableMemoryStream, ReadOnlySequenceStream, and StringStream. For example, a pipeline’s segmented ReadOnlySequence can be read as a stream without first flattening it into a new contiguous buffer.

System.Text.Json adds support for C# unions, F# discriminated unions, and JSON Lines output from asynchronous sequences. F# union support uses reflection and doesn’t support source generation, trimming, or Native AOT, so check the documented limits before choosing it for a trimmed application.

LINQ adds FullJoin, which preserves unmatched elements from both inputs. This is useful for reconciliation work, such as comparing two inventories where you need to see missing items on either side.

The libraries also expand numeric types, DNS resolution, cryptography, and tracing configuration. The library overview covers the complete set of changes and marks APIs that remain experimental.

Entity Framework Core 11 improves how your model becomes a query and how that query becomes SQL.

EF Core removes unnecessary joins in more reference-navigation and split-query scenarios, improves GroupBy translation, and translates the new LINQ FullJoin operator on relational providers that support it. These changes can simplify generated SQL while letting more queries stay in LINQ.

Complex types and JSON columns can now be used on entities mapped with table-per-type or table-per-concrete-type inheritance. Other improvements include migrations tooling and additional provider-specific JSON capabilities.

For SQL Server applications, EF Core adds support for vector indexes and approximate vector search. The SQL Server features and corresponding EF APIs are experimental. See the vector search documentation and limitations before evaluating them.

EF Core 11 requires .NET 11. When upgrading, check your database provider’s compatibility and the EF Core 11 breaking changes as well as the runtime changes.

The new dotnetup toolchain manager lets you install and update .NET SDKs and runtimes in your user account. It’s currently in public preview and helps you manage side-by-side versions and keep up with servicing updates.

.NET Skills gives coding agents task-specific guidance from the .NET team. Our .NET Skills introduction explains how to add these skills to your coding agent.

The experimental .NET MAUI DevFlow toolkit exposes a running app’s visual tree, screenshots, and interaction tools, so an agent can inspect and test the UI rather than work only from source code.

For modernization, the .NET Upgrade canvas gives you a visual view of a GitHub Copilot upgrade session. Review assessment findings and dependency compatibility, follow implementation tasks, and monitor failures and progress as the upgrade agent works. Our modernization walkthrough in the GitHub Copilot app covers the workflow from assessment through execution.

Application types are most useful when the rest of the stack understands them. ASP.NET Core supports C# unions wherever it uses System.Text.Json, including JSON request and response bodies in Minimal APIs and MVC. Generated OpenAPI documents describe the union’s alternatives with an anyOf schema.

This support applies to JSON bodies, not to route, query, header, or form binding. When multiple union cases have the same JSON shape, configure a classifier so deserialization can distinguish them. The ASP.NET Core union documentation explains those boundaries.

Validation also becomes more practical for rules that need to do I/O. Asynchronous validation works with the new asynchronous DataAnnotations APIs, so a validator can await a database lookup or a remote call instead of blocking a thread. Minimal APIs and Blazor forms can use this support.

OpenAPI 3.2 is now the default for generated documents. Among other improvements, it lets the document describe the per-event payload of a server-sent event stream. If a client generator or another part of your toolchain doesn’t yet support 3.2, you can explicitly select an earlier document version.

For long-lived connections, SignalR authentication refresh lets a connection refresh authentication without reconnecting. Blazor Interactive Server components can receive the updated identity and react when claims change.

Blazor adds capabilities for both statically rendered pages and interactive applications.

The new CacheView component caches the rendered output of a component subtree during static server-side rendering. On a cache hit, Blazor replays the markup without creating those child components or running their lifecycle methods. That gives you a smaller unit of caching for expensive, mostly static sections of a page.

Interactive Server apps can opt into automatic circuit pause when a browser tab becomes inactive. After a configurable delay, pausing frees the server memory and SignalR connection held by an inactive user. The feature requires an additional package and explicit configuration; upgrading doesn’t silently change your application’s circuit behavior.

Forms gain client-side validation for static server rendering and asynchronous validation support. QuickGrid and Virtualize add ways to move to a particular item, useful when returning a user to their place in a large collection.

Blazor also adds components for agentic user interfaces. The new Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.AI package includes components for streaming chat, tool rendering, approval flows, and shared UI state. Basic chat works with any IChatClient. For advanced experiences with a remote agent, the AG-UI .NET integration carries streamed responses, tool events, approval requests, and shared-state updates between an ASP.NET Core agent endpoint and the Blazor UI.

The Blazor AI components package is experimental and remains prerelease throughout .NET 11. It’s an opportunity to try the direction and give feedback, not a stable part of the ASP.NET Core shared framework. See the Blazor AI components documentation.

The AG-UI .NET SDK introduction covers the protocol’s client and server APIs. Build Agentic UI with the new Blazor AI components puts them together in a working app.

See what’s new in ASP.NET Core for the Blazor features, configuration examples, and compatibility details.

.NET MAUI in .NET 11 makes CoreCLR the default runtime across its platforms. The move to CoreCLR brings mobile and desktop apps onto the same runtime foundation as other .NET workloads. On Apple platforms that don’t allow JIT compilation, the runtime uses precompiled code and the interpreter rather than generating native code at runtime.

Native AOT is an opt-in publishing alternative; its Android support remains experimental. Review the platform-specific runtime guidance and test your app’s libraries when choosing a deployment model.

The UI development loop gets attention, too:

These are meaningful runtime and handler changes, so exercise navigation, custom handlers, and trimmed publishing as part of your upgrade. The optional Microsoft.Maui.Controls.Compatibility package no longer ships; apps that still reference it need to migrate off it.

.NET MAUI follows its own support policy, separate from the .NET runtime’s STS and LTS lifecycle.

For new Windows desktop apps, WinUI 3 is the recommended native UI framework, pairing C# and XAML with modern controls and the Fluent Design System. It’s delivered through the Windows App SDK, which also makes modern Windows APIs available to existing WPF and Windows Forms apps.

To add UI tests, see UWP and WinUI 3 apps: UI testing with MSTest.

Windows Forms in .NET 11 adds an opt-in modern visual styles mode that you can select at the application or control level. It also adds ways to react to system visual and accessibility settings, defer revealing a form until it’s ready, and suspend painting during bulk updates.

These controls let you introduce refreshed rendering where it makes sense for your application instead of treating a framework upgrade as an automatic visual redesign.

WPF updates focus on reliability, including fixes for Fluent window backdrops, window manager failures, and weak-event cleanup. Self-contained Windows publishing also includes a native dependency needed by apps that load mixed-mode WPF assemblies.

Install the .NET 11 SDK. Use a compatible, up-to-date Visual Studio 2026 installation, or Visual Studio Code with C# Dev Kit.

You can replace the generated program with either of the C# examples above to try the new language features.

For an existing application, plan the upgrade across your development and deployment environments:

Upgrading from .NET 8 or .NET 9 .NET 8 and .NET 9 reach end of support on November 10, 2026. Move to a supported release to continue receiving fixes. Choose .NET 11 for the latest capabilities, or .NET 10 if its LTS lifecycle fits your requirements better. See the .NET support policy for dates and servicing requirements.

If you’re upgrading from a .NET 11 release candidate, update to the final SDK, runtime, and compatible package and workload versions rather than staying on a prerelease build.

.NET 11 is the work of over 7,000 contributors. Thank you to everyone who contributed code, tested previews, reported issues, and helped us make better design decisions throughout the year. Your feedback across the previews and release candidates helped shape what we’re shipping today.

The .NET ecosystem continues to grow, with more than 589,000 unique packages on NuGet and over 1.1 trillion package downloads.

Download .NET 11, try it with an application you know well, and join us at .NET Conf to learn directly from the people building it. Share your results and questions in the .NET GitHub discussions.

.NET is the free, open-source, cross-platform framework for building modern apps and powerful cloud services.

Pochettino next? The last 10 managers to go from an international job to a Premier League one

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Like a stuck record, Manchester United are looking at Mauricio Pochettino again as they seek a replacement for Michael Carrick.

He caught their attention before with his impressive work at Tottenham Hotspur, having originally made his name in the Premier League with Southampton.

But these days Pochettino isn’t managing day-to-day. He’s currently in charge of the USA, who he led to the World Cup round of 16 this summer.

If United go for Pochettino as their Carrick successor, they’d have to hope he could make a seamless transition back into club management. It’s a different ball game, let’s be honest.

But it’s a transition several managers have made before. Working backwards, here are the last 10 managers to be appointed by a Premier League club when their previous job was in charge of a national team.

After the disaster of England’s Euro 2016 exit to Iceland, the experienced Hodgson remained out of work for more than a year.

You wouldn’t have blamed him if he said that was that. He was approaching his 69th birthday at the time and could look back on a well-travelled career.

But Hodgson had more in the tank. In September 2017, he got the call from Crystal Palace – the club whose academy he had played in as a youngster decades before.

Palace had just got rid of Frank de Boer after his shocking start to Premier League management. They needed someone who knew what they were doing, fast.

Over the next four years, Hodgson did a fine job of stabilising Palace. Taking over when they were bottom of the league, he steered them to 11th place by the end of the season.

A 12th-place finish followed in his second season, before back-to-back campaigns where they finished 14th. Palace were midtable safe bets.

They moved on from Hodgson in 2021 as bigger ambitions took hold, but they turned back to him for a second spell in charge between March 2023 and February 2024.

What is it with Palace and appointing these former long-serving (ahem) England managers? Gareth Southgate to replace Pierre Sage, anyone?

Allardyce was chosen as Hodgson’s successor for England in 2016, but famously only got to manage one game before having to step down after a newspaper sting.

Seemingly impressed by his 100% win rate, Palace chose him as their replacement for Alan Pardew that December.

The Eagles were hovering just above the relegation zone at the time. Allardyce briefly took them into it but lifted them out by the time it mattered, guiding them to 14th in the final standings.

He announced he was leaving at the end of the season and intended to retire, but ended up managing three more clubs.

Former Juventus boss Conte got the itch to get back into club football after two years in charge of his native Italy, who he took to the quarter-finals of Euro 2016.

By then, he already knew the Chelsea job was waiting for him after an announcement in April.

Conte took to his new role like a duck to water. Implementing his tried and trusted back three system, Chelsea stormed to the Premier League title at the first time of asking.

However, the second season of Conte’s spell at Stamford Bridge wasn’t as successful. They went one better than the season before by winning the FA Cup, but fell to fifth place in the league.

It was enough for Conte to get the sack, even though Chelsea had to pay out more than £20m to him in compensation.

After a year out, he returned to Italy to take charge of Inter Milan in 2019. Chelsea stuck to the Italian theme by naming Maurizio Sarri as his successor.

Before Conte’s arrival, Chelsea had seen out the 2015-16 season with Hiddink as their interim manager after parting ways with Jose Mourinho.

It was Hiddink’s second stint with Chelsea. His previous had been in 2009 alongside his duties as Russia manager and this time came half a year after his reign with the Netherlands ended.

He enjoyed a 14-game unbeaten run in the league to lift Chelsea away from bottom-half obscurity and into midtable. They finished 10th, but failed to replicate the FA Cup-winning success of his first spell.

Hiddink never managed a club again. His next job was in charge of China’s U23/Olympic team before he finished his career with a spell in charge of Curacao.

Ranieri was in charge of Greece for just four games, winning none, before being sacked in November 2014. When Leicester appointed him the summer after, most people wondered what they were thinking.

Probably not of winning the Premier League, that’s for sure. But we all know what happened next. Legendary.

Advocaat has been a well-travelled manager who you probably remember more from being in charge of countries than clubs, unless you’re a fan of one of his former employers like Rangers.

He’s spent time in charge of the national teams of the Netherlands (in women’s and men’s football), the UAE, South Korea, Belgium, Russia, Serbia, Iraq and Curacao. Some CV.

Advocaat only ever had one job in the Premier League and that was with Sunderland in 2015. He was hired four months after leaving his post with Serbia, where he was in charge for just four games.

After replacing Gus Poyet, Advocaat steered Sunderland to safety in the top flight and threw in a derby win over Newcastle for good measure.

He said he was going to retire after the season ended, but committed to Sunderland for another year instead. He didn’t last for that full season, though, instead resigning in October.

Back in 2014, in the early years of the post-Fergie fallout, they were searching for a new manager after David Moyes hadn’t worked out and Ryan Giggs had seen out the 2013-14 season as player-manager on an interim basis.

United confirmed Van Gaal as their chosen new manager that May, just before he led the Netherlands to the World Cup semi-finals.

Van Gaal guided United to fourth place in his first season in the Premier League, which was better than seventh from the season before.

But in his second season, he could only come fifth. United also got knocked out of the Champions League in the group stage.

He went out with something to celebrate by winning the FA Cup, but he was sacked two days after the final.

It wasn’t until 2021 that he managed again, returning for a fresh spell in charge of the Netherlands that lasted until the end of the 2022 World Cup.

After sacking Avram Grant – who himself had also been appointed after an international job, with Israel, in 2007 – Chelsea installed Luiz Felipe Scolari as their new manager for the 2008-09 season.

He hadn’t managed a club since 2001. In the time between, he had a year in charge of Brazil – winning the World Cup in 2002 – and five in charge of Portugal.

After reaching the Euro 2008 quarter-finals, Scolari started work with Chelsea.

It didn’t last. With Chelsea fourth in the table, he was sacked in February 2009, to be replaced by Russia boss Hiddink (who retained his international role at the same time and so isn’t counted again here).

Hodgson’s England appointment wasn’t his first rodeo in international football. Far from it, in fact.

His most recent spell with a national team before was with Finland, with whom he spent the best part of two years.

His work there put him in contention for the Northern Ireland job when Lawrie Sanchez left for Fulham in May 2007, but ironically, Hodgson ended up replacing Sanchez at Craven Cottage instead later that year.

He began work just before the New Year, tasked with taking Fulham out of the relegation zone.

Not only did he achieve that, but Hodgson later got Fulham into Europe and made them Europa League runners-up in 2010 before being headhunted by Liverpool.

On the back of winning seven trophies with Rangers, McLeish got the gig as Scotland boss in January 2007.

He was only in charge of them for 10 games and had a decent record, winning seven, but Scotland failed to qualify for Euro 2008 under his watch.

When Birmingham City came calling in November 2007, it piqued his long-standing interest in testing himself in the Premier League.

The Scottish FA refused Birmingham’s request to speak to him, but McLeish resigned anyway and took the job.

Although he started well with a win away at Tottenham, McLeish’s side were relegated by the end of the season. He stayed with them in the Championship and ultimately led them back into the top flight at the first time of asking.

A top-half finish followed in 2009-10, before Birmingham won the League Cup the following season. But that season ended in another relegation and he chose to step down, before controversially joining rivals Aston Villa.

Don’t Store Crypto on iPhone, Ledger CTO Says

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Ledger Chief Technology Officer Charles Guillemet has recently warned cryptocurrency holders not to store critical wallet information on their iPhones, as the security threat is on the rise.

In his recent post on X, the Ledger executive highlighted the fact that vulnerabilities that can leak important data are now becoming more and more accessible for hackers to exploit.

"Unfortunately this kind of vulnerability used to be very expensive and reserved to high potential attackers," Guillemet tweeted.

However, he pointed out that we're now on the verge of seeing the costs of such vulnerabilities go down to zero, meaning that it could become very easy for cybercriminals to exploit them on a massive scale.

"Simply don't store any valuable in your mobile. Especially not your crypto seeds," the Ledger CTO cautioned.

His words came in response to an urgent security alert published by crypto exchange Binance over a vulnerability that was found in Apple's iOS operating system.

In particular, cryptocurrency exchange Binance reported a vulnerability, dubbed worse_query, which can allow malicious applications to gain access to files of other applications installed on the same device.

As specified by the exchange, this security flaw affects iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2.

This means that crypto wallet recovery phrases, private keys and login credentials can be at risk, as they may be stored in accessible local files.

Binance underscored that this concern isn't exclusive to the exchange's app alone, while adding that the vulnerability can have an impact on the entire crypto space.

The exchange recommended that users uninstall apps that were downloaded from untrusted sources, update their operating systems and avoid installing any unauthorized software.

Binance also advised users to transfer their funds to newly created wallets on clean devices if they think their wallets may have been compromised.

Apple published new security updates on Nov. 9. However, it's not clear if they are enough to fully patch this vulnerability. Still, there have been no confirmed crypto thefts specifically linked to the exploit.

Guillemet's warning comes a month after Ledger's major security incident involving compromised hardware wallets.

Back in October, French cryptocurrency hardware wallet maker Ledger admitted that some hardware wallets distributed by its Southeast Asian reseller CryptoBilis had been tampered with.

It turned out that hackers managed to install complex hardware implants in legitimate Ledger devices. With that, hackers were able to intercept users' recovery phrases when people were setting up their Ledger wallets for the first time.

As shown by Ledger's investigation, malicious implants were capable of capturing the 24-word recovery phrase that appears on the Ledger device screen and then passing it to attackers via a cellular connection.

Blockchain sleuths estimated that this incident might have led to more than $86 million in crypto losses. However, Ledger itself didn't confirm the total amount.

Police 'are trawling through dodgy Fire Stick sellers' bank accounts to contact illegal viewers' of Premier League football and films

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A former detective has revealed the lengths authorities are going to in order to crack down on the use and sale of 'dodgy Fire Sticks'.

Law enforcement organisations across the UK are taking a tougher stance on these devices – and how they are used and distributed – with multiple people having been convicted and jailed in recent years for selling and supplying them.

The devices, which often cost anywhere between £30 and £100 – in comparison with around £30 a month for a Sky Sports subscription – give customers access to a range of premium TV services without paying, most notably in a football sense with access to live matches.

But Rav Wilding, a former detective who recently appeared on the BBC Morning Live show, has sent a fresh warning to individuals that use them.

Wilding said: 'People are buying them online usually on the black market and then they're getting all these services for free essentially.

'But it's a crime. It's not allowed. Owning the stick itself is not a crime, but using it for this purpose is.'

A former detective has revealed the lengths authorities are going to in order to crack down on the use and sale of 'dodgy Fire Sticks'

Wilding went on to say that along with a crackdown on sellers through house raids, authorities are now looking through their accounts to find out in further detail who is using the devices.

They then often contact those users in a new step, with it estimated that around four million people or households have got these Fire Sticks at the moment.

Wilding added: 'All of them are using them illegally. They're going for the distributors. They're going for the sellers. They are having a lot of success. But actually, once they've got the seller, they're going through their accounts.

'They're seeing who has been paying the money for these illegal things, and if they've been paying them for these sticks then people should possibly expect a conversation or two if the police want to know why they've been paying them money for this.'

Wilding revealed that this included checking bank statements to find out the information of individuals who have been paying for such a service, and authorities have been joined by subscription service providers in their clampdown attempts too.

'They (subscription service providers) are losing money to this, so they have been reaching out and are taking action against those people using them illegally as well,' he explained.

'So I wouldn't say it's something you should do. It is actually a crime and it can have a nasty fine, a huge fine and also a custodial sentence for users and sellers of these things.'

Rav Wilding appeared on BBC Morning Live and sent a fresh warning to users of the devices

Daily Mail Sport has previously reported FACT is playing a leading role in the crackdown on the devices, with the anti-piracy organisation liaising with police forces across the country.

And Kieron Sharp, chairman of FACT, added: 'We have a wide range of enforcement options - from issuing cease and desist notices to "knock and talk" visits - through to full criminal prosecutions where appropriate.

'We will take robust action against those involved - not only targeting sellers and distributors, but also those facilitating and profiting from the illegal activity.'

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/finance/hims-hers-reports-8904m-q3-revenue-tightens-2026-outlook" hreflang="en">Hims &amp; Hers reports $890.4M Q3 revenue, tightens 2026 outlook</a>

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →
Hims & Hers reports $890.4M Q3 revenue, tightens 2026 outlook" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Digital health and wellness company Hims & Hers tightened its full-year 2026 revenue outlook after missing Wall Street analysts’ revenue and earnings estimates.

Andrew Dudum, co-founder, chairman and CEO, pointed investors toward the increasing scale of Hims & Hers' pharmacy and lab infrastructure. That growth is driving efficiencies for the company, as is its artificial intelligence native experience, he said.

“That infrastructure positions us to launch treatments of increasing complexity, including testosterone replacement therapy this quarter and our Peptides Plus offering later this month,” Dudum said.

Peptides have been a focus for the company as of late, with Dudum telling investors last quarter it plans to offer already-permitted peptides, like sermorelin and NAD+.

Dudum said Hims & Hers’ long-term strategy focuses on “three critical commitments:” being a leader in metabolic health domestically and globally, leading major markets globally and being "at the forefront of category expansion.”

“Each of these reinforces the other, and together they describe how Hims & Hers becomes the global destination for consumer health,” he said. “We are investing aggressively against all three, because this opportunity is growing quickly, and we believe it will become more valuable every quarter.”

The company reported $890.4 million in revenue in Q3, up 49% compared to revenue of $599 million the year prior, according to financial results published Monday. Net loss was $33 million, compared to $15.8 million in profit the same quarter a year ago.

Chief Financial Officer Yemi Okupe said revenue excluded $18 million from the “scaling of membership revenue” in the company’s weight loss offering, but will be recognized in the fourth quarter.

“This revenue deferral has no impact on free cash flow generation,” Okupe noted.

Adjusted EBITDA was $82.4 million compared to $78.4 million the year prior.

Ethan Feller, stock strategist at Zacks Investment Research, told Fierce Healthcare the company’s revenue was “roughly what Wall Street expected,” adding that U.S. “growth sped up to 21%.”

“But the company lost 14 cents a share against an expected gain of 6 cents, its third big miss in a row,” Feller said. “It is also keeping less of each sale, as gross margin fell to 57%, down from 74% a year ago, as more of the business shifts to lower-margin brand-name weight-loss drugs.”

Feller added the company’s “growth story is still a real long-term tailwind.”

“Demand for weight-loss drugs is strong, and having both Novo and Lilly on the platform helps,” Feller said. “But as long as margins keep shrinking, the stock will likely stay under pressure, until either the price falls to a level that reflects the lower margins or the company shows it can turn a steady profit.”

As a result, Hims & Hers tightened its full-year 2026 revenue guidance to a range of $3.13 billion to $3.15 billion and reduced its adjusted EBITDA guidance to a range of $257 million to $277 million.

“This is a reflection of the more aggressive strategic pricing and marketing actions that we expect to take in the U.K. and Germany, and the weight loss membership deferral noted above,” Okupe said.

The earnings report comes on the heels an announcement that the company struck a deal with Eli Lilly to offer its branded obesity treatments directly through the Hims & Hers platform. The drugs were previously only available to patients if they left the site and went to Lilly's direct-to-consumer platform, LillyDirect.

“Nearly 3 million people across four continents rely on us to access care,” Dudum said. “That trust has made us a valuable partner to global pharmaceutical leaders like Novo and Lilly, and positions us to keep bringing the most innovative treatments to our customers as they come to market.”

Dudum added the company is planning to launch “flexible multi-month subscriptions” across its assortment to make it easier for customers to address health goals.

“We will continue to prioritize partnerships across the industry, with the goal that whenever an innovative therapy reaches the market, our customers can access it here,” Dudum said. “That assortment will sit alongside an AI-native experience that will soon be available to nearly every weight loss customer on our platform.”

Toyota brand Scion is back and it's going to sell a kei truck in America

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

When Toyota first introduced the Scion marque in 2003, it had one primary goal – sell cars to the younger generation. Its cars were cheaper and available in fun colors with fun names like “High Voltage Yellow” and “xPRESSO.” When I was a kid in the early 2000s, I often dreamed of one day owning a tC in Black Cherry Pearl and enjoyed lovingly calling xBs “toaster cars.” Over half of new Scions sold were to people under 35, and the tC boasted the youngest average buyer age in the entire industry at a sprightly 29. Their sales numbers peaked in 2006, but dropped nearly 75% by 2010. After sustained financial struggle, Toyota retired the marque in 2016. But now, a decade later, everyone’s favorite toaster brand is seemingly back.

Today, Toyota announced the official revival of Scion, but instead of being a quirky and budget-conscious line, they are becoming Toyota’s cornerstone of curiosity. Now called “Scion by Toyota,” it is being launched as a “freedom lab” where drivers can, and are encouraged to challenge conventional ways of thinking about mobility through design, vehicle concepts, and features.

Their first production vehicle is the KEI-01, an ultra-compact utility truck, otherwise known as a kei truck. Toyota will be using its existing manufacturing infrastructure in Indiana to “produce” them for sale in the American market. “Produce” is in quotation marks because the KEI-01 is quite literally a rebadged Toyota Pixis, which is a rebadged Daihatsu Hijet Jumbo Cab. Based on its initial imagery it is seemingly also still right-hand drive, and there’s no word on if a LHD variant will be available in the future.

With its friendly little 660cc engine and CVT transmission, the KEI-01 was certified to meet American low-speed vehicle safety standards, meaning it has a limited top speed of less than 25mph and is only legal on roads with a posted speed limit of less than 35mph. This makes it not really usable as a daily driver, but keeps it in this beautiful sweet spot where it has a shocking number of commercial and agricultural uses.

The KEI-01 has a 600-pound payload (which means cargo and passengers) and a three-way fold-down cargo bed, making it a well-equipped workhorse for use on farms and in warehouses. It’s only about 5 feet wide and 11 feet long, which makes it great for use as a delivery or work vehicle in dense, urban areas like New York City. Believe it or not, it also has 4WD, which means it can traverse uneven terrain in more rural areas with ease. And don’t worry if it’s hot outside, because heat and air conditioning are standard.

It’s worth noting that, while Kei trucks are currently sold new in the U.S. as “mini trucks” through independent distributors, this one is sold directly by Toyota. Before you rush to your local Toyota dealer to place a deposit, I’m going to have to burst your bubble a bit. Initially, the KEI 01 is only going to be available in a low-volume, niche pilot program entirely focused on fleet usage beginning in late 2027. Depending on performance and owner feedback, availability of the KEI-01 may eventually be expanded, but no further information is available from Toyota at the time.

A post shared by The Autopian (@theautopian)

Two other mobility concepts accompany the KEI-01 in the Scion by Toyota lineup, the SxS Concept and the BXE Concept.

The SxS Concept was unveiled at the 2025 SEMA Show as a purpose-built side-by-side all-terrain vehicle (click here for our coverage). Developed by Toyota North America, the off-road concept is outfitted with a turbocharged four-cylinder from the Tacoma making in the realm of 300hp. You won’t have to worry about your turbo spool scaring away local fauna, as it is equipped with a “Silent Mode,” which allows the driver to switch from the boisterous internal combustion system to a much more civilised fully electric driving mode. Unlike the KEI-01, the SxS Concept is still just that – a concept, with no current plans to have a version available to the public.

Last, but not least, is the BKE Concept. The BKE concept is entirely focused on personal mobility in the outdoors, filling the gap between the utility of the KEI-01 and the pure performance of the SxS Concept. Its small form factor and rugged tires make it an ideal off-roader for traversing narrow paths while being portable enough to stow away when not in use or easily tote around in the back of a car.

I feel like the other two are here just to pad the line up because just selling a kei truck might make investors whinge.

As a DIEHARD Scion fan this is the most disappointing use of this name.

Ok, now bring the Sienta over as a Scion, please!

Xbox Vs. PS5: GTA 6 Specs Revealed, And These Are The Biggest Differences

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

"Pushing both platforms to their absolute limits"

Rockstar Games has today lifted the lid on a bunch of specs for both the Xbox and PS5 versions of GTA 6, revealing some of the biggest differences (and similarities) between playing the game on each platform.

The most notable bit of information is that every single console runs the game at 30FPS. Regardless of whether you're playing on Xbox Series S, Xbox Series X, PS5 or even PS5 Pro, the max you're getting is 30 frames per-second.

"Grand Theft Auto VI has been fully developed from the ground up to provide the best possible experience for the current generation of consoles, targeting 30 frames per second while pushing both platforms to their absolute limits to bring the vast and rich world of Leonida to life — while maintaining the density and visual fidelity of all its environments and inhabitants."

There will obviously be resolution differences between each platform, but Rockstar has yet to reveal these details. Expect the Series S to fare the worst, the PS5 Pro to fare the best, and the Series X and PS5 to be pretty much equal.

Rockstar does say that GTA 6 "plays best" on PS5 though (and let's not forget that Sony appears to have a marketing deal in place with Rockstar Games for this release), highlighting the differences between the PS5 and Xbox versions as being "high-fidelity haptic feedback" for the PS5 DualSense controllers, improved spatial audio rendering, and bizarrely, improved load times. That last one is surely just a minimal advantage at best!

Grand Theft Auto VI for PS5 includes high-fidelity haptic feedback fully integrated across the game on DualSense controllers and adjustable via accessibility settings, improved spatial audio rendering so all in-game sounds take full advantage of Tempest 3D Audio, and improved load times, making Grand Theft Auto VI play best on PlayStation 5 consoles.

Beyond that, the PS5 Pro version will benefit from improved clarity via PSSR along with ray-traced shadows.

However, the Xbox version has one massive benefit in the form of Xbox Cloud Gaming. It seems Xbox will be the only platform offering cloud streaming for GTA 6 at launch, making it an exclusive of sorts - at least for now. The bizarre bit is that it only appears to be for consoles, rather than other devices like PCs and mobiles.

The Xbox and PS5 versions will also support Remote Play (allowing you to cast your game to another device from your console). Nothing has been said about Quick Resume yet on Xbox, but we assume it'll be supported as well.

Ultimately, GTA 6 is bound to run great regardless of whether you buy it on Xbox or PlayStation, and the "plays best on PS5" differences mentioned by Rockstar Games appear to be minor advantages rather than major ones.

We can't wait for footage to start appearing online next week — especially for the Xbox Series S version — and here's hoping it's going to live up to the ridiculous hype that's been fueling the past decade. Not long to wait at all now!

New Windows 11 update fixes File Explorer menu mess – and patches an exploited flaw

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Tech > Services & Software > Operating Systems > Windows > Windows 11

With the November Patch Tuesday rollout, you can finally customize your right-click menu in Windows 11. It fixes 1,094 security flaws, too.

Windows 11 users who’ve struggled to navigate and use the right-click menu in File Explorer now finally have some relief. With the November Patch Tuesday Windows update, you’re now able to customize the menu to add and remove specific commands and apps so that only your favorites appear.

As usual, the monthly update is mandatory, which means it should automatically download and install on your PC. To check, head to Settings and select Windows Update. If the latest update — labeled “2026-11 Security Update (KB5132061) (26300.9974)” -– pops up as installed with a pending restart, just reboot your computer. Otherwise, click the button to check for updates and allow it to download and install.

Also: You can change Windows settings directly from a search now

One caveat: Microsoft’s monthly updates fully support Windows 11 26H2 and 25H2. But support for 24H2 ended last month, at least for the Home and Pro editions. Windows 11 24H2 Enterprise and Education will continue to get monthly security updates, though not regular non-security preview updates, while Windows 11 23H2 Enterprise and Education will receive no more updates after this month.

If you use Windows 11 Home or Pro, be sure you upgrade your system to 26H2 to continue receiving the necessary support and updates.

First and foremost in the latest update is the new customizable right-click menu.

The context-sensitive right-click menu is a handy way to quickly access key settings, features, and apps, both in File Explorer and on the desktop. But there’s always been a flaw in its basic design. The more apps you install, the more cluttered and disorganized the menu becomes. That was especially problematic in previous versions of Windows. The old-style, classic menu would grow so long that it would be difficult to navigate and let you find the item you wanted.

With Windows 11, Microsoft introduced a more modern and pared-down menu. But this version can also get crowded. Plus, it may not display every command you need, so you often have to revert to the older classic menu. You’re then stuck dealing with two separate versions of the same menu, leading to an even more confusing and cumbersome experience.

Also: Windows 11 can run better on PCs with 8GB now, Microsoft says

The ideal solution is a menu that you can customize yourself, and that’s what we now have, at least that’s what is now rolling out. Microsoft calls this one a gradual rollout, which means you may or may not get it right away.

To see if and how this is available, open File Explorer and right-click on a folder or file. If the new menu has reached your PC, you’ll see an entry for Customize menu. Select that to view all the available options for tweaking it. Alternatively, head to Settings, select Personalization, and then click the entry for Context menu.

There, you’re able to turn certain file actions on or off, such as Send to, Print, Create shortcut, Copy as path, and Rotate image. You can also choose which apps appear all the time, either in the main menu or in a submenu. Plus, additional options let you decide where certain commands and actions appear and whether you can still access the old-style menu.

Though the customizable right-click menu is the star of the update, Microsoft has added other surprises as it strives to make Windows more reliable and user-friendly.

Also gradually rolling out, Cloud rebuild is a new recovery option that lets you reinstall Windows 11 via cloud-based system images and drivers downloaded from Windows Update. This tool can help you recover Windows when it won’t load and you don’t have a USB drive or your own custom image. The downside is that your files won’t survive. But if you regularly back them up as you should, you can restore them after Windows is back in business.

To start Cloud rebuild, go to Settings, select System, click Recovery, and then select Rebuild PC. Your PC will then restart into the Windows Recovery Environment (WinRE) to kick off the cloud rebuild. If Windows can load, your computer should automatically enter WinRE mode, where you can then choose the cloud rebuild. You should need to make sure your PC is online via a wired or wireless connection.

The November update also enhances or fixes other Windows features and apps.

File Explorer will now more clearly and consistently show you the names, locations, and dates of files when you search for them. Phone Link will preview the text in a recent message if you hover over it. The AutoPlay dialog box that appears when you connect a device like a phone or camera has been given a more modern makeover. The Privacy & security page in Settings now groups together related controls for easier viewing. Plus, there are changes to the Taskbar, Magnifier, Narrator, Xbox Mode, and Live Captions.

And that then brings us to the security fixes.

This month’s update resolves 1,094 security vulnerabilities, according to Microsoft, with 63 of them rated critical and one already exploited in the wild. That’s fewer than October’s record of 1,400 patches. But it still represents a hefty number as Microsoft and other vendors continue to rely on AI to find and fix security holes.

Also: Windows finally gets a built-in internet speed test tool – how to try it

The exploited flaw points to a spoofing vulnerability in Microsoft Outlook. Here, a remote attacker who sends you a malicious email and convinces you to open it could cause Outlook to connect to a network controlled by that attacker. In this case, though, you would not receive any warning. Further, the exploit might reveal your username and password to the sender, potentially allowing them to impersonate you.

Bittensor gains 3% after Tencent’s AI launch – Can subnet fees lift TAO?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Is Q4 shaping up to be the most bullish quarter yet for decentralized AI?

The whole of the AI market has come under increased scrutiny recently. The release of earnings from big tech companies such as Amazon shows just how expensive expanding into AI can be. Their stock dropped nearly 8% in a short span of time, causing over $200 billion to be wiped off their value.

While the stock has since recovered most of its losses, gaining over 11% so far in November, the sell-off was still a major reality check for investors.

This raises an important question: Could growing concerns over Big Tech’s AI spending become a catalyst for decentralized AI networks?

Recently, Bittensor has seen an uptick in momentum as one of its subnets (Chutes) was chosen by Tencent as a launch partner for their new flagship AI model HY4.

The key takeaway? Tencent has a combined 1.439 billion monthly active users on their Weixin and WeChat platforms.

Chutes charges $0.525 per million input tokens and $1.5625 per million output tokens. This shows how decentralized AI providers are competing on cost while reaching larger markets.

Notably, the impact is also reflected in the market.

Bittensor’s [TAO] price has risen by more than 3% to trade at $220. This comes after the partnership with Tencent, amid increasing worries about the increasing costs of AI in Big Tech. This raises an important question: Could AI be emerging as a key driver of crypto’s growing divergence from the broader market?

Strategically, Bittensor is positioning itself at the center of the growing AI trend.

The affordable services it offers are becoming more attractive, especially with the new partnership with Tencent that gives access to a whole ecosystem with close to 1.5 billion monthly active users.

This can increase Bittensor’s exposure, allowing for more decentralised AI adoption. So, to track how this trend could impact the wider AI crypto sector, TAO will be a key token to watch.

Interestingly, this momentum is also starting to show up on-chain. The Bittensor-based decentralized AI platform Almanac (SN41) generated $109,440 in fees during October. This is the first time it has exceeded $100k in fees.

The interesting part? Almanac burned 100% of these fees to buy back TAO and SN41 alpha.

Meanwhile, Engy (SN53), a Bittensor subnet dedicated to providing verifiable AI inference for open-source models, has already spent $63,000 on buybacks and token burns.

Together, these developments highlight how growing subnet revenue is translating into direct buying pressure for TAO.

However, the key test will be whether this momentum can translate into real market demand. TAO’s recent 3% rally suggests that investor interest may already be picking up.

So, if more subnets continue generating revenue and using it for token buybacks and burns, Bittensor could build a stronger fundamental case and become one of the strongest plays in the decentralised AI sector heading into 2027.

Tencent’s partnership with Chutes puts Bittensor in front of nearly 1.5 billion users, boosting its exposure to the AI market.

Growing subnet revenue and token buybacks are supporting TAO demand, while its 3% rally signals rising interest in decentralized AI.

New TMNT: Splintered Fate Update Adds Even More Reasons To Play In Co-Op

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Super Evil Megacorp has been steadily pumping out new content for its TMNT Hades-like, Splintered Fate, ever since launch, and today, the team has added a bunch of new reasons to embark on your roguelike runs in co-op.

The new Shell Squad Co-Op Update goes live today, and as its name suggests, it's all about the multiplayer fun. This free patch adds 24 new co-op powers, eight new co-op tools and eight new co-op masteries, so you can bring the heat to Shredder and the Rat King with even more skills when playing with friends.

The update also includes improved flame visuals, options for character swapping when playing co-op online or locally, and improved Lucky Dice communication.

Here are a few examples of the new powers and tools added in the update:

24x new co-op powers (3 per power tree). Examples: - Passing the Torch - The first player you touch while affected by Rapid Fire also gains its effects, for as long as you have Rapid Fire active. - Shared Warmth - When a player consumes a Splinter's Revive, heal all players. - Chain Reaction - Players' Specials have a chance to trigger a Chain Lightning, dealing Utrom damage per link.

8x new co-op tools (1 per power tree). Examples: - Bubble Trouble - Spawn a bubble on you and the farthest player. The bubble deals Water damage and Freezes enemies, and grants bonus Water damage to players. - Tether Beam - Tether to the closest player. Enemies caught in the beam will take Utrom damage every second. - Shadow Pact - Grant yourself and the closest player a Dark Shell. Each enemy defeated extends it.

8x new co-op masteries (1 per character). Examples: - Donatello - When using your Tool, the closest player becomes invulnerable. - Raphael - When any player lands a critical hit, they have a chance to Multi-crit instead. - Michelangelo - When using your Special, you and the closest player gain a Torrent Strike stack. - Leonardo - When activating your Special, the closest player gains Light Shell.

That's not all the news on the Splintered Fate front, either. The dev also announced its next paid cosmetics DLC: the Trench Coat Pack. Once again, it's another pretty self-explanatory one here, with all the Turtles (and the additional DLC characters) donning the team's iconic trench coats. This one is also available today, so there's no waiting around here, either!

17 goals in 14 games: Middlesbrough flop and ex-Leicester City man hits stunning form with new club

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Leicester City had Kelechi Iheanacho on their books from 2017 to 2024, bringing him in from Manchester City after his eye-catching break into senior football. The Nigerian striker was a mainstay for the Foxes, playing 232 times for the club across all competitions.

He struggled for consistent form for the most part, but did net 19 goals and provide seven assists in 39 games in a prolific 2020/21 campaign. Iheanacho was unable to rediscover that level, and after his departure from the King Power Stadium, he endured a tough half-season loan at Middlesbrough in 2022. He flopped as he managed one goal in 15 games.

A stint at Celtic in the 2025/26 season followed, before Iheanacho moved to Turkey with second tier side Bursaspor in the summer. And, the former Leicester City and Middlesbrough man has hit stunning form there.

Albeit against a lower level of opposition, Iheanacho is in the best form of his life with Bursaspor. The 30-year-old has scored a remarkable 17 goals in 14 league games this season.

The 59-cap Nigeria international went on a run of scoring in 12 consecutive league games, which included four braces. He fired a blank in a 3-0 win over Bandirma Sport at the end of October but followed that up with a return to scoring ways against Antalyaspor last week.

Iheanacho’s success comes from Bursaspor’s brilliant chance creation. He has taken 56 shots in total, equating to an average of four a game (FotMob). As such, with his finishing and experience, it is little wonder that the striker is enjoying such a goalscoring resurgence after his spells at Leicester City and Middlesbrough.

Bursaspor’s goal is to return to the Turkish top flight for the first time since 2019. They have rebuilt over the last two years after dropping all the way to the third tier and having a player of Iheanacho’s pedigree on a three-year deal displays their ambition.

As Iheanacho rediscovers his scoring touch at Bursaspor, Middlesbrough and Leicester City have found new star strikers of their own this season.

At Boro, Will Lankshear has been one of the Championship’s signings of the season. He has 15 goals in 16 games across all competitions, drawing interest from Newcastle United and catching the eye of England manager Thomas Tuchel.

Meanwhile at Leicester City, Emil Riis has hit form after joining for around £2m in the summer. The Foxes’ form has been inconsistent, but the Danish striker has eight goals in 11 games for the club.

Andy Robertson was left with a ‘headache’ after two Liverpool games last season that he ‘hated’

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Andy Robertson has said that there were two Liverpool matches in particular last season that he ‘hated’, having been left with a ‘headache’ after both.

The Reds were beaten twice by Galatasaray in the 2025/26 Champions League, with a pair of 1-0 defeats at RAMS Park in the league phase in September and the round of 16 first leg in March (Arne Slot’s team won the return fixture 4-0 a week later).

Both matches were almost clones of one another, with the Merseysiders conceding an early goal and being unable to restore parity after putting in a subdued performance.

Now of Tottenham Hotspur, Robertson reflected on the two visits to Istanbul last season and how he was left with a migraine from the incessant intimidation from the natives.

The Scottish left-back said (via Fanatik): “I hated playing against Galatasaray last season. The Galatasaray away game was terrible. They booed non-stop throughout the entire match. Believe me, you leave the match with a headache.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

The city of Istanbul has left an indelible mark in Liverpool’s European history from the epic 2005 Champions League final against AC Milan, but recent visits to the Turkish capital have been bruising.

The Reds twice relinquished a lead to lose 3-2 away to Fenerbahce last week, and the two trips to play Galatasaray last season were galling, to say the least.

Pat Nevin justifiably claimed that the 1-0 league phase defeat ‘could have been worse‘ given the nature of our defending that night, and John Aldridge described the repeat of that scoreline in the knockout phase eight months ago as a ‘horrible watch‘.

Slot had warned about the febrile atmosphere of RAMS Park prior to the latter fixture, remarking: “Sometimes you wonder if this could be done by human people”, and for a player of Robertson’s character and experience to be so rattled by the intensity of the home support is quite telling.

With Galatasaray currently 18th in the league phase standings, it’s not inconceivable that they and Liverpool could meet at some point in the knockout rounds yet again next spring, but we’d imagine that the Reds have had their fill of visits to Istanbul after the past 14 months.

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The two companies are exploring new AI editing capabilities.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Adobe has announced an expanded partnership with Google at Adobe MAX 2026, and while the announcement is heavy on AI jargon, there implications for creatives could be interesting.

Perhaps most exciting is a collaboration with Google DeepMind to develop new AI editing capabilities for Photoshop. The companies are exploring a customised Google image model that could allow users to make near-real-time edits using text prompts, switching between variations without disrupting their workflow. In theory, that could make generative editing feel less like a frustrating cycle of prompting and waiting, and more like working with a traditional Photoshop tool. The catch? It's still exploratory, with no release date announced.

“Adobe’s mission is to empower everyone to create, and our AI innovations are helping more people express their ideas and create in ways that amplify their voice, while maintaining creative control,” said Anil Chakravarthy, president and incoming CEO of Adobe. “Our expanded partnership combines Google’s frontier AI with Adobe’s industry-leading creative and productivity tools at global scale, enabling us to deliver differentiated experiences and bring Adobe’s innovations to more people across Adobe and Google products.”

Meanwhile, Adobe is bringing its creative tools to Google's AI Mode in Search, potentially allowing users to go straight from searching for inspiration to creating something without opening a separate app.

Adobe is also expanding its integrations with Gemini and Gemini Enterprise, enabling users to enhance images, customise designs and work with PDFs through conversational prompts. For designers churning out multiple versions of campaign assets, for example, that could be a genuine time-saver.

Perhaps the most interesting aspect of the announcement, though, is what it suggests about Adobe's direction. Rather than expecting creatives to work exclusively within its own apps, it seems Adobe increasingly wants its tools accessible wherever people are already working, such as Google Workspace.

JSX Adds Two Nonstop Routes From New City

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

JSX announced Tuesday that it will begin flying to New England for the first time in February 2027.

The carrier will operate from Jet Aviation’s terminal at Hanscom Field in Bedford, Massachusetts, northwest of Boston. Flights to Teterboro Airport in New Jersey will begin Feb. 8, with four flights on weekdays, two on Saturdays, and three on Sundays.

JSX markets the route as New York City service and requires passengers to sign up for its free Club JSX membership to book.

Service to Miami-Opa Locka Executive Airport will follow on Feb. 11, with one flight each day on Mondays and Thursdays through Sundays. JSX will use 30-seat aircraft on both routes.

“We’re excited JSX has chosen Hanscom to operate these flights to the New York and Florida markets,” Hanscom Field Director Chris Willenborg said in a statement.

JSX outlined its plans for Hanscom Field at an advisory commission meeting in September. At the time, the carrier was targeting a January launch and had also proposed flights to Naples, Florida. Naples was absent from Tuesday’s announcement.

Inside Washington Dulles’ New Concourse E, Light Years Ahead Of United’s “Temporary” Gates

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Concourse E at Washington Dulles International Airport is now open for business and it’s a beautiful terminal that stands in stark contrast to the “temporary” C and D Concourse that United Airlines has used since the mid-1980s.

I was present for the ribbon-cutting yesterday, which featured United executives Andrew Nocella (Executive Vice President and Chief Commercial Officer) and Toby Enqvist (Global Chief Operations Officer) as well as Virginia Governor Abigail Spanberger and U.S. Senator Tim Kaine and a number of other elected officials and airport leaders. Steven Bradbury, President Trump’s Deputy Secretary for Transportation, also spoke.

While everyone is going to play nice at these sort of events, I was happy to see Republicans and Democrats talking together about investing infrastructure, one of the many opportunities for bipartisan collaboration as the new Congress assumes power. There’s reasonable discussions over cost overruns and the massive time and money drain this project has been already, but it has to be done and maybe just maybe everyone can come together to accelerate this project and hold everyone more accountable.

But my main point here it to showcase the beautiful new terminal. It’s world class without a comparison, but we cannot help but compare it to the C/D Gates and it’s truly night and day; the difference between first world and third world.

First, it’s very easy to reach. Take the train to the C gates stations and now instead of walking a half miles back to the terminal, you can simply walk upstairs to the new terminal:

There’s plenty of natural light and and the Untied Club is conveniently located on at the top of the stairs/escalators on the right side. Many new concessionaires are slated to open, with several highlighting local food and beverage options from Northern Virginia.

And yes, the mobile lounges remain. Passengers arriving on an international flight will still be directed to a mobile lounge and driven to the mid-field immigration facility. Eventually, a new facility will be opened in Concourse E.

This is phase one. The terminal expansion will continue in 1-2 more phases and the entire concourse will not be done until the end of the decade, at the earliest (sad, but predictable).

I wrote about the new United Club here. It’s posh and United will also build a new Polaris Lounge in this Concourse.

Bottom line: it’s a bright new day for Washington Dulls and the new terminal expansion is a fitting upgrade for the capital city of the United States.

Shiba Inu Heads for Recovery as Netflow Turns Red With Over 114 Billion SHIB

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Shiba Inu has begun to show signs of a potential recovery after multiple days of severe price correction as the broader crypto market continues to face increasing sell pressure.

While momentum appears to be shifting, the latest on-chain data from the crypto analytics platform CryptoQuant shows that buyers are beginning to dominate the Shiba Inu market once again.

Although Shiba Inu is still struggling amid the prolonged market volatility as its price continues to trade in the red territory, its exchange activity is providing a different signal.

According to the data, the Shiba Inu exchange network has declined modestly by around 1.37% over the last day, sitting at -114,647,400,000 SHIB as of the time of writing.

The negative netflow balance indicates an increase in the demand for the leading meme token among investors as it means that the number of SHIB tokens that have been moved out of exchanges for buy purposes over the last 24 hours has substantially outweighed the number of SHIB tokens that flowed into exchanges in attempts to sell.

Apparently, this means that traders have bought more tokens than they have sold over the last day, positioning Shiba Inu for a bullish price reversal after the sharp price declines seen in the past days.

Although Shiba Inu is still showing mixed price action as it is yet to respond to the bullish exchange activity seen over the last day, market participants are regaining confidence in its potential price move.

While its price movement still remains slow, its hourly price chart shows that momentum is gradually changing as it has turned green, suggesting that Shiba Inu might be on the verge of recovery.

Some local news outlets are losing physical newsrooms

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

All the latest headlines and insights from the world of digital journalism and publishing. Our daily newsletter is a must-read from newsrooms to executive suites.

We will never sell your address. Prefer a weekly email?

We have sent a confirmation link to . Click it and you are on the list. If it has not turned up in a minute or two, it may be in your spam folder.

When the Covid-19 pandemic normalized remote work, a second trend caught on: shutting down physical newsrooms.

A report from Northwestern University’s Local News Initiative, published Tuesday, looks at the cultural losses that news outlets face when their staff stops working together under one roof. Here’s LNI contributor and Columbia College Chicago journalism professor Jackie Spinner:

As pandemic-era safeguards that necessitated working from home waned, some have championed the technological advances that allow many journalists to report, edit and publish from basically anywhere with stable Internet access. But the cost savings from a smaller or nonexistent physical presence isn’t the only consideration. Less clear-cut is the impact on the elements of journalism that never appear on budgets, such as the training absorbed from looking over someone’s shoulder or the institutional knowledge gained and passed by working in proximity to others over time.

During the pandemic, several news outlets downsized their offices — from city high-rises to co-working spaces — due to financial constraints and new hybrid work schedules.

On Tuesday, I spent time looking for the local news outlets that have foregone or reduced office space and opted for primarily remote work in recent years. Here are some:

Are there any I’m missing? Let me know. And read the full piece here.

An average of 24% of respondents across nine countries said they'd seen a label noting that content was made with AI in the last week, and 20% had seen a bullet-point AI news summary.

Arsenal Could Benefit as PSG Step Away from Ibrahim Maza Transfer Race

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal could have been given a helping hand in their quest to sign Ibrahim Maza. Paris Saint-Germain reportedly pulls out of talks with Bayer Leverkusen’s attacker.

The Gunners are just one of several clubs keeping an eye on the exciting young talent in Germany. However, with some of the biggest clubs in Europe keen on the prospect of adding the player to their attack, it might have made it difficult for them.

PSG looked like genuine contenders for the signing of Maza. The French champions having reportedly had talks with both Maza and Leverkusen.

It seems that PSG are not interested anymore, according to Fichajes. They have ended negotiations with the player and Leverkusen because they were unhappy with how the player’s representatives and Leverkusen have conducted themselves during the talks.

This could be great news for Arsenal, but it doesn’t mean they have full control of the situation.

The withdrawal of a club of PSG’s calibre is a benefit for Arsenal. They had seemed well on their way to landing Maza. Now, they will pursue him without the pressure of competition.

Maza has emerged as a good signing for clubs looking to improve the flair in their attacking third. Leverkusen will have noticed this and may be tempted to sell him if they get a decent offer.

Arsenal might also determine if Maza would be a valuable asset for their team in the long run. They must ensure that he can adapt to their attacking strategies.

They will not be the only one interested in Maza. Although PSG may be willing to let him go, there are many other clubs interested in the player.

Leverkusen will also want to get the best deal out of the situation. If they are willing to negotiate, Arsenal need to think if it would make financial sense to do so.

Finally, it will come down to what the player wants. Arsenal would need to persuade the player that the Emirates is the right place for his progression and that they can provide the right route for his aspirations and ambitions.

This may be a step in the right direction for Arsenal.

If Maza is still a target for the Gunners, then they may have a better chance of getting it done given PSG’s new stance. But they will still need to come to an agreement with Leverkusen and convince the player to move to north London.

Lewis Hamilton visits Egypt’s Rafah crossing and Gaza aid facilities

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Seven-time F1 world champion tours border area and logistics warehouses operated by Egyptian Red Crescent

Lewis Hamilton has visited Egypt’s Rafah border crossing with the Gaza Strip to meet organisations sending humanitarian aid to the territory.

The seven-time Formula One world champion has spoken out about conflict and humanitarian crises in the region, and has called for peace. He has frequently posted on social media about children killed in Israeli airstrikes in Gaza and called for a ceasefire.

“I think we need to make sure we’re pushing to free Congo, free Sudan, free Syria, free Palestine. I think there’s a lot of peace that I’m hoping we get to at some stage,” Hamilton said in October.

A ceasefire agreement reached last year has halted major combat operations in Gaza, but the amount of aid reaching the territory continues to fall far short of the agreed-upon minimum of 600 trucks a day.

Israeli forces have continued to advance, expanding their control from 53% of the territory to at least 60%, an independent commission of UN experts said last month.

Airstrikes also continue, and Israeli fire along the advancing front has killed at least 196 Palestinians, including 18 women, 32 boys and 11 girls, the commission said.

Hamilton toured Egypt’s Rafah border crossing with Gaza before visiting logistics warehouses operated by the Egyptian Red Crescent, which facilitates the entry of aid to Gaza.

At the crossing, Hamilton greeted aid workers with “Assalamu alaikum” a widely used Arabic greeting meaning “peace be upon you.” He shook hands and briefly spoke to workers and officials.

Khaled Megawer, the governor of North Sinai, where Rafah is located, said the visit was intended to help Hamilton learn about Egypt’s efforts to send aid to Gaza.

The visit comes after Hamilton made his 400th race start on Sunday at the São Paulo Grand Prix at Interlagos.

The first black F1 driver, he made his debut with McLaren in 2007. He is only the second F1 driver to start 400 races after his former teammate Fernando Alonso, who achieved the milestone in 2024.
