Need more AI in Creator Studio? Apple may charge you for it

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

With its new $50 AI usage packes, Apple is the latest company to introduce paid model for AI access.

There are plenty of AI subscriptions out there, but Apple’s AI features are among the few still accessible by simply purchasing a device. Now, Apple is shifting its business model by offering a one-time $50 purchase option to grant server-based AI capacity in some apps, starting with Creator Studio apps.

Apple is introducing a one-time purchase option for AI Usage Packs via app updates to Creator Studio. If you want expanded access to AI features in Final Cut Pro, Pixelmator Pro, the iWork Suite, and Logic Pro, you’ll need to pay.

Also: ChatGPT Plus vs. Google AI Pro: Two $20 plans, very different perks

The $50 fee applies to extra usage blocks and kicks in when you exceed predetermined limits in these apps. Instead of a recurring fee, Apple is hoping the one-time fee for intelligence usage packs will help the company offset some of the cost of AI. Users can buy a pack for Logic Pro now, and the other apps are expected to get it soon.

“Usage Packs may offer the option to prepay for the use of certain AI-based Services and represent the amount you have paid in advance for use of the respective Services,” according to Apple.

Apple’s one-time payment breaks away from what most companies are doing. Most companies offering AI tools try to offset the steep cost of running large language models (LLMs) by charging users for paid subscriptions. For example, Samsung is rumored to be planning such a subscription for Galaxy AI usage, including access to features that are currently free.

Also: The best tablets of 2026: Lab-tested recommendations

The Creator Studio apps are the only ones getting the Usage Packs for now. The legal terms for intelligence Usage Packs explain that these packs would be available in “AI apps,” which include not only Final Cut Pro, Compressor, Logic Pro, MainStage, Pixelmator Pro, Motion, but also Pages, Keynote, Numbers, Freeform, and other apps.

You’re not required to purchase the Usage Packs to enjoy the core functionality of the apps in the Creator Studio. However, the fact that the Usage Packs are to be applied to popular apps like Pages and Freeform begs the question of whether Apple plans to put a paywall on features that are currently free.

Some of the features for which you may be required to purchase Usage Packs once you exceed your limits include the generation of images, shapes, presentations, and presenter notes, as well as some assistant features. Usage limits reset each month.

Also: Alexa Tablet 12 Pro review: Forget everything you knew about Amazon tablets

You can view your usage status for each of the applicable apps on your iPhone or iPad by tapping on More (or the button with three dots), then Intelligence Features, and Show Usage Status. On a Mac, it’s available by accessing Intelligence Features from the menu bar of the app you’re using, then clicking on Show Usage Status.

Intelligence usage packs expire after 12 months, but they roll over each month if left unused.

70+ Dogs Rescued From South Korean Meat Farm as 2027 Ban Approaches

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

We just sent a welcome email your way with a quick overview of what to expect (including those vital recall alerts).

Dogster is reader-supported. When you buy via links on our site, we may earn a commission at no cost to you. Read about privacy.

Nearly 30 dogs have arrived in New York after being rescued from a dog-meat farm in South Korea. Their journey toward adoption is beginning, but with the country's ban still months away from taking full effect, the work is far from over.

A plane ride to New York probably wasn't on these dogs' bingo cards. Neither, for that matter, was a soft bed, a full bowl of food, or the chance to be someone's beloved family member. After spending their lives confined to cages, even the simplest things may seem exciting and new.

On October 1, nearly 30 dogs were rescued from a dog-meat farm in Gangjin, South Korea, that once bred and slaughtered as many as 1,000 dogs each year. This was part of a larger rescue operation by Humane World for Animals that saved more than 70 dogs from the property.

For the organization, the operation marks its final large-scale dog-meat-farm rescue in South Korea ahead of the country's ban, which takes full effect on February 7, 2027. Humane World for Animals has rescued nearly 3,000 dogs from farms since 2015, making this a significant milestone.

And yes, it's a reason to celebrate. But before we get too comfortable patting ourselves on the back, there's a much bigger picture to consider. Getting these dogs out was a huge victory, but there are still thousands at risk.

When the rescue team arrived at the farm in Gangjin, in South Korea's South Jeolla Province, they were met with disturbing scenes. Rows of rusting cages, abandoned collars scattered on the ground, and skeletal remains served as a gruesome reminder of the dogs that hadn't been so lucky.

It's a hard reality to come to terms with, especially if your own dog is currently hogging the sofa, demanding snacks, or staring at you as though you've ruined their life by being five minutes late with dinner. The minor dramas faced by our beloved pets are a world away from what these poor dogs endured.

Humane World for Animals has reported finding mixed breeds and dogs of different sizes during its rescue work. The survivors included Jindos, a breed native to South Korea, a husky mix, and smaller dogs, including a spitz mix. In other words, any dog could end up on the menu.

In this most recent rescue, a concerned citizen alerted a local animal protection group, which contacted Humane World for Animals and the authorities. Officials found that the farm had broken South Korea's Animal Protection Act. The farmer, a man in his 80s, agreed to hand over the dogs and promised not to take part in the dog-meat trade again.

More than 70 dogs were rescued. Nearly 30 travelled to New York, another 12 went to a rehabilitation center in Montreal, and some mothers and their puppies remained in South Korea because they were too young to fly safely.

Rescuing dogs from situations like this isn't as simple as just opening a cage and booking the next available flight. International transport requires veterinary checks, vaccinations, documentation, and careful consideration about which animals are fit to travel. The priority is getting them out safely, not rushing them onto a plane for the sake of a happy headline.

South Korea passed its Special Act on the Termination of Breeding, Slaughter, and Distribution of Dogs for Consumption in January 2024. Its key prohibitions will finally take full effect on February 7, 2027, making it illegal to breed, slaughter, or sell dogs or dog meat for human consumption. But the transition is already underway.

In May 2026, South Korea's Ministry of Agriculture, Food and Rural Affairs reported that about 82% of the country's 1,537 registered dog-meat farms had closed. Over a thousand farms closed is something worth celebrating, but it means 272 farms are still operating.

Another consideration is that closing a farm doesn't automatically save the dogs living there. A business can shut down, the gates can close, and the paperwork can be signed, but where do all those dogs go?

According to Humane World for Animals, government figures indicate that of approximately 393,857 dogs counted on farms that had been closed, only 1,121 were adopted or transferred to public shelters. That's about 0.3%.

Itâs understood that most of these dogs were sent for slaughter or moved to other farms. So, while the closure of dog meat farms is good news, itâs not a true victory if these dogs are simply moved on to face the same fate. The ban is a huge step forward, but a law is only as good as its enforcement.

The real goal isn't just to close the farms. It's making sure the dogs are actually safe.

Getting out of the farm is the first big hurdle for these dogs. Now comes veterinary care, recovery, and the not-so-small task of learning how to live in a completely different world.

Humane World for Animalsâ rescue protocols include veterinary exams, vaccinations, respiratory screening when needed, and the health documents required for international travel. Each dog has their own needs. Some may need treatment for skin problems, wounds, dental disease, or nutritional deficiencies.

The second, and perhaps most challenging, hurdle for these dogs is adapting to a whole new way of life. Just because a dog has been saved doesn't automatically mean a dog feels safe.

Imagine never having lived in a home, then suddenly finding yourself surrounded by doors, stairs, strange noises, slippery floors, and humans who keep trying to show you how much they love you. Even a well-meaning hug could be overwhelming. And the vacuum cleaner? Let's just say it probably won't be winning any popularity contests.

These dogs may need to learn things many pets take for granted: how to explore a room, relax around people, or understand that a hand reaching toward them isn't something to fear. Some will be curious from the start. Others may prefer to watch from a safe distance. Neither response is wrong, and patience plus a little common sense go a long way.

A quiet place to rest, a predictable routine, and the freedom to explore without being pushed can help a dog settle in. Rewarding small steps with positive reinforcement builds confidence without turning every new experience into a test.

Adjusting to a new life takes time, and there's no need to rush it. A dog who hides under the table on day one isn't a failed adoption. A dog who isn't ready for cuddles isn't being ungrateful. Theyâre figuring things out, and they need time to decide that this strange new place might actually be safe. Building trust and confidence can be a slow process.

Those familiar with the process of adopting or fostering dogs, particularly those who may not have had the best start to life, may have heard of the 3-3-3 Rule for Dogs: 3 days, 3 weeks, 3 months. Itâs a simplified interpretation of the timeline many dogs tend to follow when adjusting to a new home environment.

The first 3 days are about decompression and resetting the nervous system. It might look like a dog that is timid, constantly moving, hiding, or even showing signs of aggression. Some dogs adjust quickly, while others may take a little longer than 3 days. The important thing to know is that this version of the dog is not usually a good representation of their true selves.

Over the first 3 weeks, dogs will start to gain confidence and explore their new world. They are often testing boundaries, becoming familiar with patterns, and learning how to trust. Being consistent and providing predictable routines is paramount to this process.

By the time 3 months have passed, most dogs will be feeling more settled. It may still take some time for their full personality to appear, but with every passing month, they become more confident, more trusting, and less anxious.

Sometimes progress looks like taking a treat from someone's hand. Sometimes it's finally falling asleep without startling at every sound. Those moments might not make a flashy before-and-after video, but for that dog, they're a big deal.

One more thing: fear and behavioural changes shouldn't automatically be dismissed as consequences of a dog's past. Pain, illness, and other medical problems can affect behaviour, too. If a dog seems persistently distressed or suddenly starts acting differently, a veterinary check is important. We don't want to assume it's trauma when there may be pain due to a treatable medical issue behind it.

A second chance isn't just getting a dog out of a cage. It's giving them the medical care, space, and time to discover that life can be safe, and maybe even pretty wonderful.

Humane World for Animals calls the Gangjin operation its final large-scale dog-meat-farm rescue in South Korea. But that doesn't mean the work is over. The organization plans to continue monitoring the ban and supporting efforts to enforce it.

Closing farms is a huge win. Full stop. But making sure dogs aren't left behind, moved elsewhere, or subjected to illegal practices is part of making that win count. With the ban taking full effect in February 2027, the coming months will be crucial.

For the dogs already rescued, a new life is finally within reach. Veterinary care. Rehabilitation. A chance to find a home where the biggest worry is whether they get the good spot on the sofa. That's quite the upgrade.

Now, we turn our attention to the dogs still waiting in South Koreaâs remaining farms. Stopping the dog meat trade is a huge victory, but in many ways, itâs just the beginning. Saving these dogs is more than just stopping the slaughter. Itâs making sure they all get the happy ending they deserve.

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Mis à la porte par l'écurie Haas avec effet immédiat, Esteban Ocon a pris la parole ce mardi 10 novembre 2026. Le pilote français a tenu à adresser un message sobre à ses supporters ainsi qu'à l'ensemble du paddock de la Formule 1.

Cette prise de parole intervient une semaine après l'officialisation de son éviction, actée le mardi 3 novembre 2026 pour les quatre derniers Grands Prix de la saison 2026. Une rupture brutale alors qu'Esteban Ocon, 30 ans, occupait la 16e place du classement des pilotes avec 7 points, contre 20 points pour son coéquipier Oliver Bearman.

Sur son compte Instagram, le vainqueur du Grand Prix de Hongrie 2021 a publié un texte sobre, sans la moindre rancœur envers sa direction. Esteban Ocon a préféré remercier chaleureusement les personnes qui l'ont soutenu durant cette épreuve, rappelant son attachement indéfectible pour sa discipline.

Le pilote tricolore compte 199 engagements et 198 départs en Formule 1, ainsi que 4 podiums en carrière.

L'aventure s'est pourtant terminée dans la tourmente. En effet, tout s'est accéléré le samedi 31 octobre 2026 lors du Grand Prix de Mexico sur le circuit de l'Autódromo Hermanos Rodríguez. Mis à l'écart soudainement après un différend interne lors des essais libres du vendredi, le Français avait alors cédé sa place au réserviste japonais Ryo Hirakawa. Un coup dur.

Une séparation était déjà programmée fin septembre 2026, mais l'accord prévoyait initialement qu'il termine la saison. Toutefois, le directeur de l'écurie, Ayao Komatsu, a affirmé avoir pris cette décision anticipée dans le meilleur intérêt de la structure. Haas a d'ailleurs aligné le rookie brésilien Rafael Câmara, déjà engagé pour 2027, dès le Grand Prix de São Paulo le week-end du 8 novembre 2026.

Désormais sur la touche, Esteban Ocon fait face à un futur incertain. À trois manches de la fin de la saison 2026, il ne dispose d'aucun baquet titulaire confirmé pour 2027. Au-delà des doutes contractuels, sa passion et son amour pour la Formule 1 restent aussi forts que jamais.

Le prochain rendez-vous du championnat du monde de Formule 1 2026 sera le Grand Prix de Las Vegas, 21e manche de la saison, programmé du 19 au 21 novembre 2026 sur le Las Vegas Strip Circuit.

Lufthansa’s First Airbus A350-1000 Has Arrived: 299.5 Seats, Four Cabins, Munich Routes

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

In 2023, Lufthansa Group placed an order for the Airbus A350-1000 — the order was initially for 10 frames, before being increased to 15 frames the following year. The Lufthansa Group is undergoing a massive long haul fleet modernization at the moment, with both Airbus and Boeing planes on the way.

Along those lines, the first Lufthansa A350-1000 has just joined the carrier’s fleet (in a dazzling livery no less), so let’s cover all the details, including the routes the plane will fly, how the plane is configured, etc.

Between late 2026 and 2030, Lufthansa is taking delivery of 15 Airbus A350-1000s. This is only one of several aircraft types the airline is acquiring, as it’s also taking delivery of Airbus A350-900s, Boeing 787-9s, and (eventually) Boeing 777-9s.

The first A350-1000 has just been delivered today, with three more planes expected to be delivered in the coming weeks, before the end of the year. This first plane even features the special 100th anniversary livery, as Lufthansa has seven of these flying, with one representing each fleet type.

The first plane has the registration code D-AIFA, and is named “Deutschland” (since Lufthansa names all of its planes). This is a special plane for another reason, as it’s also the 700th aircraft that Airbus has delivered to Lufthansa Group. How gorgeous is this plane?!?

Here’s how Heiko Reitz, Lufthansa’s Hub Manager for Munich, describes this:

“We are proud that the Airbus A350-1000—Airbus’s most modern long-haul aircraft—is coming to Munich. The introduction of the new A350-1000 is part of the largest fleet modernization in the history of the Lufthansa Group and a key driver on the path to more sustainability. Our new flagship features 40 percent more Business and Premium Economy seats. To put it simply: The A350-1000 strengthens our premium hub in Munich.”

On a slight tangent, over the summer I saw the 100th anniversary Boeing 747-8 in Frankfurt (FRA), and I must say, I think it might be the most breathtaking plane in commercial service. Is it just me?

We’ve known that the Airbus A350-1000s will get the new Allegris cabins, including the new Allegris first class and Allegris business class. So, what can we expect in terms of the configuration? The A350-1000 features 300 seats, spread across four cabins, as follows:

As a point of comparison, the A350-900 (the non-stretched version of the plane) features 267 seats, as follows:

So the stretched version has 11 more business class seats, 11 more premium economy seats, and 11 more economy seats (11 more seats across three cabins, what a funny coincidence). The airline is still maintaining only one row of first class, which is kind of “bleh.”

You can find the Lufthansa A350-1000 seat map here.

Lufthansa Airbus A350-1000s will all be stationed in Munich (MUC), which is where Lufthansa bases all of its A350s. The first of these planes is entering long haul commercial service as of early December 2026.

Specifically, as of December 3, 2026, the A350-1000 will fly between Munich and Tokyo Haneda (HND), with the next two destinations being Shanghai (PVG) and Singapore (SIN), and those services expected to launch within weeks, though the schedule hasn’t yet been updated to reflect that.

Note that initially the flights won’t be operated by the A350-1000 daily, so there will be alternating service with the A350-900. It’s interesting that the initial route isn’t to North America, where a single plane could do a same day turn, and continuously stay in service. but I guess there’s enough commonality between the A350-900 and A350-1000, so it’s not a big deal.

As far as US destinations go, it’s expected that Chicago (ORD) and Newark (EWR) will be the first two cities to see the new plane, though there’s no exact timeline for that yet.

In 2023, Lufthansa placed an order for the Airbus A350-1000, and the first of these planes has just been delivered, and will enter service in early December 2026. The plane features 300 seats across four cabins, though still only expect one row of first class seats. The Munich-based plane will initially fly to Tokyo, Shanghai, and Singapore. I must say, Lufthansa knocked it out of the park with its centennial livery, and I can’t wait to see this plane flying.

Monaco summit adds unnecessary drama to Michael Carrick situation

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Michael Carrick arrived at Carrington for Manchester United training as normal on Tuesday morning, while his bosses were preparing to decide his future in talks on the Mediterranean.

Chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox are flying to Monaco to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe, with the BBC reporting that the pair will recommend Carrick is sacked.The Guardian has indicated representatives of the Glazer family will also be involved.

The club insists the Monaco summit was pre-planned and is not solely about the head coach and while that may be true, it hardly matters.By allowing the meeting to become public knowledge, United have turned a straightforward football decision into unnecessary drama, compounding an already difficult situation.

What does is say about Man United in 2026 that a manager is given three games to secure his job, then left to train the squad during an international break while the executives are flying off to rule on his future? It’s farcical.

The latest chapter is another muddled process.Wilcox and Berrada met at Carrington the day after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October and agreed to give Carrick three more matches.A defeat at Chelsea, a win over Roma and a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa later, they appear to have reached the conclusion many expected a fortnight ago.

If the timeline holds, the decision will be made at Wednesday’s meeting, with an official announcement expected later in the week.United reportedly want to tell Carrick face to face, which means he could spend another day or two working under a cloud. In reality, it could be two days or two weeks.

The stakes could hardly be higher. United have won once in 10 league games, their worst-ever start to a Premier League season, and sit one point off the bottom in 18th place.

Should Carrick go, attention will turn quickly to his successor.ESPN reports that United States head coach Mauricio Pochettino is the favoured candidate, while Eddie Howe has been named as Wilcox’s preferred choice. Antonio Conte has also been mentioned.

Carrick deserves better than this.Whatever the outcome, a club that wants to be taken seriously should not be settling its manager’s future in the full glare of the media while he is still out on the training pitch.

Popular gaming chair and gaming desk manufacturer Secretlab has today unveiled its new ErgoArch monitor lightbar, a new lightbar attachment for owners of its Secretlab monitor arm and a complement to its desk ecosystem. Available now, the new lightbar costs $179.

For owners or prospective buyers of the Secretlab Magnus Monitor Arm (the light bar is an attachment to this accessory), the ErgoArch provides overhead lighting for your desk and monitor, reducing reflections and glare, and smoothing out brightness contrast between your display and workspace. Monitor light bars are generally designed to reduce eye strain while you're working or gaming, while also providing some snazzy ambient lighting; the ErgoArch is no different.

Secretlab says the ErgoArch provides both ultrawide illumination and focused task lighting depending on how you set it up. The lighting itself is smart and can be powered by Nanoleaf, giving you access via your smart home app to 16.8 million colors. There's also a dedicated wireless control puck, and the lighting temperature ranges from 2700K to 6500K.

The light itself is mounted to the monitor arm via a three-point adjustment arm and is compatible with any VESA-mounted monitor, including curved displays. There's also a magnetic webcam mount attachment. Power is provided by USB-C, so you don't need a wall outlet or extension socket to switch it on.

Available globally, the ErgoArch is available to buy from Secretlab now for $179.

Allen Family Philanthropies gives $10M to hundreds of small arts groups across Washington state

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

by Kurt Schlosser on Nov 10, 2026 at 7:30 amNovember 9, 2026 at 2:56 pm

Allen Family Philanthropies and Seattle nonprofit ArtsFund awarded $10 million to 745 small arts and culture organizations across Washington state, and renewed the grant program for another $10 million next year.

The latest Community Accelerator Grant awards range from $2,500 to $25,000 and reach organizations in 37 of the state’s 39 counties. The 2027 renewal brings the program’s total to $50 million over five years.

For 2026, the program focused on groups with annual budgets under $1 million, which raised the average award by about 25%, from $10,753 last year to $13,423.

The change followed a sector analysis by Allen Family Philanthropies and SMU DataArts. It found that smaller arts groups lost an average of one staff position between 2023 and 2024, while organizations with budgets over $1 million added an average of five.

“The incredible reach of this program across our state, along with the significant findings from our report, show just how much this type of flexible support is needed to bolster Washington’s rich and diverse arts and culture sector,” said Lara Littlefield, executive director of Allen Family Philanthropies.

Of this year’s grantees, 496 are in Northwest Washington, 132 in Southwest, 67 in Eastern and 50 in Central Washington. Recipients include 12 of the state’s 29 federally recognized tribes, and more than 22% are in rural counties.

A handful of the organizations blend art and technology, including Tacoma’s Fablab Nonprofit ($25,000), Seattle-based Simulated Immersive Experimental Realities ($15,000) and Bellingham Makerspace ($2,500).

The Old Hotel Art Gallery in Othello, in Adams County, has received a $25,000 grant each year since 2024. The gallery used the money to pay staff a living wage, to keep operating until its annual fundraiser, and to renovate its historic building, which now brings in rental income.

Gallery director Samantha Copas said the 2025 award “truly kept our doors open.”

Allen Family Philanthropies, formerly known as the Paul G. Allen Family Foundation, was founded in 1988 by the late Microsoft co-founder and his sister, Jody Allen. Arts and culture is one of its core program areas, along with youth and the environment. Last year the foundation committed nearly $7 million to eight Seattle Center nonprofits, including KEXP and the Museum of Pop Culture, to expand youth access to the arts.

ArtsFund will hold a virtual webinar in the spring for organizations interested in applying for 2027 grants. Details will be posted here.

Coventry City midfielder Jack Rudoni has attracted significant interest from several Premier League clubs ahead of the January transfer window.

GTA 6 Will Be Playable On Xbox One Through Xbox Cloud Gaming

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Rockstar also confirms GTA 6 will run at 30FPS

Update [Tue 10th Nov 2026, 4:03pm]: According to The Verge's Tom Warren, he believes GTA 6 will only be playable on Xbox Cloud Gaming on consoles, with PC and mobile excluded. Xbox Wire recently posted the three ways to play GTA 6 on Xbox. We've updated the article to reflect this.

Original Story: Buying current-gen Xbox hardware isn't cheap these days, and with GTA 6 is now confirmed to be running 30FPS on Xbox Series X|S, what chance would an Xbox One have of running the game, even if it had launched for previous generation tech?

Well, thanks to Rockstar's pre-load notes, we now know GTA 6 will be playable on Xbox One through Xbox Cloud Gaming at Xbox Series S graphics settings. This means those of you who can't upgrade to Xbox Series can still play GTA 6 on November 18th November 19th!

"The game will also be available via XBOX Cloud Gaming on XBOX Series X|S and XBOX One consoles, utilizing XBOX Series S graphics settings. And as with all cloud streaming, connection speeds and other technical factors may influence game performance in ways that would not impact local gameplay. GTAVI will also fully support Remote Play on PlayStation and XBOX."

You'll of course be at the mercy of Xbox's fresh Cloud Gaming time caps, limiting how long you can stream games depending on your Xbox Game Pass subscription. You can purchase additional time beyond your allotted amount, and if you're still stuck on Xbox One, topping up your cloud gaming hours could work out cheaper than buying an Xbox Series console. A free ad-supported version of Xbox Cloud Gaming is also currently in testing.

However, there may be a catch. According to Tom Warren, it appears that playing GTA 6 via Xbox Cloud Gaming will be restricted to Xbox consoles. If you were planning on streaming the game on PC or mobile, you may need to think again.

"I also incorrectly assumed it would be like every other Xbox Cloud Gaming title and support phone, PC, web, and TV streaming too. GTA 6 is unlike any other game, so clearly an exception has been made here to restrict to just Xbox consoles"

In a recent Xbox Wire post, Microsoft adds weight to Warren's claims, stating the three ways you can play GTA 6 on Xbox:

But back to Rockstar's notes, the Scotland-based dev did indeed confirm that both Xbox and PlayStation devices will target 30FPS whilst pushing the hardware "to their absolute limits" to play GTA 6. My advice would be to give your Xbox a good clean before release. You don't want your console overheating just when the game finally comes out!

"Grand Theft Auto VI has been fully developed from the ground up to provide the best possible experience for the current generation of consoles, targeting 30 frames per second while pushing both platforms to their absolute limits to bring the vast and rich world of Leonida to life — while maintaining the density and visual fidelity of all its environments and inhabitants."

XRP Price Plunges to $1.05 After a Hot CPI Report: Will the Support Stand?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Ripple XRP fell to $1.05 after a hot CPI report. After 7 of the last 10 hot reports, XRP was higher a day later. Can $1.05 hold?

Ripple XRP fell to $1.05 on Tuesday within five minutes of the U.S. October consumer price index (CPI) report, which showed XRP USD price rising faster than economists expected.

XRP crypto traded at $1.0797 when the report came out at 8:30 a.m. in New York, or 13:30 UTC. By 13:35 UTC it had touched $1.0500 on Binance, a 2.8% drop and its lowest price since August 19. It has since bounced to about $1.07. Bitcoin fell with it, dropping to $73,525 in the same five minutes.

The drop puts XRP back on a level it has tested before. Between late June and mid-August, XRP traded around $1.05 for weeks. The level held the first time and broke the second.

So will the support stand this time, and what do the CPI numbers tell us?

BREAKING: October CPI inflation rises +0.4% month-over-month, above expectations of +0.3%.

Core CPI inflation rose +0.3%, also above expectations of +0.2%.

BLS will not be releasing year-over-year figures due to the October 2025 government shutdown.

— The Kobeissi Letter (@KobeissiLetter) November 10, 2026

Prices rose faster than forecast in October. The Bureau of Labor Statistics said consumer prices rose 0.4% in the month, against the 0.3% economists expected.

Core prices, which leave out food and energy because they swing the most, rose 0.3%. The forecast was 0.2%. Core inflation has now run at about 0.3% for three months in a row.

The report is missing one usual figure. The bureau did not publish a yearly inflation rate because it never collected prices in October 2025, during the government shutdown. That leaves the monthly numbers to carry the whole report.

Those monthly numbers matter for the Federal Reserve. Fed Governor Christopher Waller said in a speech this morning that he already leans toward a 0.25 percentage point rate hike in December. If core CPI came in around 0.3% again, he said, a 0.5 point hike “should be considered”.

Core CPI did come in at 0.3%. Before the release, traders saw almost no chance of a hike that large. Higher rates make cash and bonds pay more, which tends to pull money out of risky assets like XRP.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Next Crypto to Hit 1 Dollar

The first move after a hot report has often faded. We measured XRP’s price on Binance in the 24 hours after each of the last 24 CPI reports, from October 2024 to October 2026.

Ten of those reports had core inflation of 0.3% or higher, like today’s. After seven of them, XRP was higher 24 hours later. The median move was a 1.4% gain.

The record is weaker this year. Of the four hot reports in 2026, XRP was higher a day later only twice. After the last one, on October 14, it fell 4.4% in 24 hours.

The difference this time is the Fed. A hot report that only confirms a small hike is easier for traders to shrug off. One that puts a larger hike on the table, as Waller laid out, gives sellers more to work with.

EXPLORE: XRP Price Forecast: Ripple at Risk of Losing Key Support, $1 Next?

The level has a history this year. XRP has spent two long stretches around $1.05 since June.

Today’s low of $1.0500 sits right on that zone. The first test of a level often holds. Each new test tends to leave fewer buyers there.

XRP trades at about $1.07 on Binance after the CPI drop, down 2.8% in 24 hours and 17% in a week. It is 71% below its July 2025 record of $3.65.

On the daily chart, where each candle covers one day, XRP sits well below both of its exponential moving averages (EMAs), averages that give recent prices more weight. The 50-day EMA stands at $1.294 and the 200-day EMA at $1.350.

For Ripple XRP, the first test of $1.05 has held so far. The daily close decides it: a close above $1.05 keeps the support alive, while a close below it opens the way to $1.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Top Solana Meme Coins to Bid

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

One Liverpool player has been told by the current coach of his national team that he needs to ‘get back into shape’ if he’s to earn an international recall.

The most recent of Federico Chiesa’s 51 caps for Italy came two months before he joined the Reds from Juventus in August 2024, and the forward has struggled for game-time at Anfield under both Arne Slot and Andoni Iraola.

So far this season, the 29-year-old has been given a mere two minutes of matchday action by the Basque, coming on at the end of the defeat to Manchester City after recovering from a back injury in the early weeks of the campaign.

There have been reports that Liverpool are increasingly open to selling their number 14 in January if the right deal can be struck, and Azzurri head coach Roberto Mancini has told the player what he must to do in order to get back into his country’s squad.

The former Man City boss said (via Liverpool Echo): “As far as Federico is concerned, I have already spoken with Federico several times, including recently.

“Right now, he isn’t playing and needs to regain his fitness and get back into shape; but he is a 29-year-old player, still in the prime of his physical maturity, so I believe he can return with us very soon.”

Mancini is leaving the door open for a potential Italy recall for Chiesa in the future, but it isn’t helping the forward’s case that he’s hardly getting a look-in at Liverpool.

He’s only been in the matchday squad for four of our 16 matches so far this season and has now been leapfrogged by Lewis Koumas in the pecking order up front, with Iraola demonstrably preferring the Welsh youngster over the 29-year-old.

The Azzurri boss travelled to England a few weeks ago in the hope of seeing long-time injury absentee Giovanni Leoni in action, which suggests that he has the Reds defender firmly in mind for the national team when the teenager is back playing again.

Mancini hasn’t forgotten about Chiesa either, but the message from the tactician is clear – our number 14 needs to regain match sharpness, and right now his chances of doing that at Liverpool seem nonexistent.

With that in mind, it wouldn’t surprise us if the popular Italian forward were to depart in January, but not before winning the affection of Kopites and playing his part in our Premier League triumph under Slot two seasons ago.

From chaos to close: Holly Mabery says agents need to quell confusion

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

eXp Realty chief brokerage officer Holly Mabery told NAR NXT attendees to separate headlines from actionable local impacts. She said clearer, story-driven communication and stronger CRM follow-up can help agents win share even if 2027 sales fall below 4 million.  AI Summary

In Holly Mabery’s view, 2026 has been a year filled with chaos. The year started off full of optimism and promise and much like many new year’s resolutions quickly turned. But, Mabery, who serves as eXp Realty’s chief brokerage officer, believes agents can turn the chaos into closed transactions.

“You see a bunch of headlines and does anybody feel like they just want to go back to bed? And then it is like how do I stay ahead? What does that look like? What do I say to people, consumers, team members?” Mabery said during a session at the National Association of Realtors 2026 NXT conference over the weekend.

For agents, brokers and team leaders feeling overwhelmed by it all, Mabery said it’s first important to remember that they are not going through this alone and that other agents and brokers are asking themselves the same questions. From there, the key to navigating the chaos, according to Mabery is figuring out what the most important things are and making them relevant to the person sitting in front of them.

“I can’t teach advanced geometry to a six-year-old. But I can show them the difference  between a square and a triangle,” Mabery, a HousingWire Woman of Influence, Insider and Vanguard award winner, said. “The more you and I make things relevant and connect the dots for people, it becomes easier. But also I find that in connecting the dots, that trust layer opens up and everybody exhales.”

In markets and environments currently experienced by real estate professionals, Mabery said it is easy to allow the chaos to turn in confusion.

“When we have confusion, then there is no action as we lose opportunities. The more people are confused, the more they are afraid to move. It is like being stuck in really deep, thick mud,” she said.

To prevent this from happening with their agents and clients, Mabery said brokers and agents need to recognize what has happened and explain it using no jargon terms and then translate it to their specific market or situation by employing a noise versus action filter. This will allow them to understand what a national headline means for them and what actions they may need to take as a result.

Mabery used an example of the Federal Reserve’s rate hike in September, noting that most people feel this is a buyer problem and would have a conversation with their buy-side clients about how this impacts them and how they may need to change their offer strategy. But, Mabery said in her view it is actually a seller problem.

“This is a seller problem because now, when less buyers feel like they can take action, it falls to the seller to take action,” Mabery said. “What are you willing to do in this market to actually make a difference to get people into your home and writing an offer?”

When you provide consumers or even agents with information like this, Mabery says it enables them to feel empowered and hopeful.

“As you look at 2027, I’m not scared for 3.8 million transactions, I’m just hunting for my piece of it,” Mabery said.

With agents and brokers seriously contending with the possibility of a sub-4 million home sales environment in 2027, Mabery said it is important that agents and brokers find news and data sources they can trust, that they get back in touch with past-clients in their CRM and that they work on their story-telling skills.

For Mabery, keeping client-facing messages short, personal and story-driven will enable agents to better connect with consumers and build trust.

“The relationships you and I have are making all the difference in the world,” Mabery said. “The more confidence you build the more you inspire people to take action. When people are confused they stop taking action and we don’t sell anything and that is what we saw in 2026.”

Despite this, Mabery noted that people still move and buy homes due to life events, inviting real estate professionals into their inner circles during some of the most exciting and trying times, but in order to be invited into these moments agents need to build trust in advance.

“Take away the chaos and talk about what is next,” she said. “I want you to calm the fears as we go forward. The more you share your knowledge, your value goes up. And if I don’t take action, is anybody hurt? No. But if I do, I get to help them.”

First Ski Descent of Meru Central

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Tiphaine Duperier and Boris Langenstein of France have notched another Himalayan first ski descent, this time down the southwest face of Meru Central (6,310m) in India’s Garhwal Himalaya. The skiers are still surprised by how lucky they were.

Duperier admitted they started the expedition with low expectations after several projects earlier in the fall all failed.

“Snow conditions had been unforgiving,” she wrote. “We figured there was only a 3-in-10 chance of skiing the southwest face of Meru.”

But when they got there, the mountain was, they added, “generous” with them.

The three-peaked Meru massif, located in the Gangotri Glacier region of the Garhwal Himalaya, lies between Shivling and Thalay Sagar. It has three peaks: 6,660m Meru South, 6,310m Meru Central, and 6,450m Meru North. The ski descent line is in purple. Photo: Duperier/Langenstein

“We spent six days of reconnaissance and three [days] climbing the peak, [thanks to] an unprecedented stroke of luck in finding a way through the icefall and perfect snow in the upper section,” she explained.

The icefall on Meru Central. Photo: Boris Langenstein

The icefall at the base of the peak was indeed one of the most complex passages. The pair climbed a 15m pitch of WI4 and left a safety rope, which they also used to rappel down after the ski descent, Duperier told PlanetMountain.

Otherwise, the pair of regular expedition and skiing partners climbed alpine-style and pretty fast, reaching the summit in less than eight hours. According to their research, they completed the second-ever ascent of the southwest face of Meru, after an Indian team in 2023.

At the summit on Meru Central. Photo: Duperier/Langenstein

The skiers estimate they covered 1,500m on their descent, with some sections reaching 50 °. Snow conditions were good overall, hard but with a good grip.

“We skied like little monkeys on the wires in Rishikesh,” Duperier posted on social media. “It was just incredible, truly beautiful!”

While the climbers rappelled down the icefall onto the glacier below, the ski descent itself had no rappels or interruptions.

Duperier barely had time to return home to France to pick up a Piolet d’Or for the first alpine-style ascent of the Schell Route on the Rupal Face of Nanga Parbat, followed by the first ski descent from the 8,125m summit.

Tiphaine Duperier at the Piolets d’Or ceremony. Photo: Piotr Drozdz

Angela Benavides graduated university in journalism and specializes in high-altitude mountaineering and expedition news. She has been writing about climbing and mountaineering, adventure and outdoor sports for 20+ years.

Prior to that, Angela Benavides spent time at/worked at a number of local and international media. She is also experienced in outdoor-sport consultancy for sponsoring corporations, press manager and communication executive, and a published author.

ExplorersWeb is part of AllGear Digital's portfolio of media brands.

This bear’s big butt is so beautiful, it just won an award

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 10, 2026 3:13 PM EST

Yes, bears are big. Depending on the time of year and sex, the average black bear (Ursus americanus) weighs between 90 to 500 pounds. On average brown bears (Ursus arctos) can weigh between 600 to 900 pounds. However, one female black bear named Volverine is a true fat bear queen this year. The U.S. Forest Service team at Tongass National Forest in southeastern Alaska have awarded her with the 2026 Big & Beautiful Award for her heft.

The rangers say that this mother bear is a “true icon,” who will often pose on a rock for a long time, seemingly allowing the visitors to Anan Wildlife Observatory to take photos. She is regarded as one of the most beautiful bears at Anan, with a chest patch shaped like the letter V. Volverine is also on the bigger end of the black bear spectrum, with a potato-shaped body, and an especially round butt. Her fishing skills made her one of the largest bears of the 2026 season—and a dominant matriarch.

“Our rangers love to see a fat female bear because that could mean cubs next year!” the rangers write. “Bears have what’s called delayed implantation, meaning they mate in the spring, and IF they get fat enough, the embryos implant in the fall.”

Wishing Volverine and all of the bears the best this winter season.

The rangers at Tongass National Forest are currently awarding the Anan Bear Awards to the 39 bears spotted at the Anan Wildlife Observatory in Alaska over the summer.

Tongass is the largest national forest in the United States, and covers most of southeastern Alaska, including the state’s capital city of Juneau. The area includes Mendanhall Glacier (a popular destination for Alaskan cruises), hiking trails, eagles soaring above, and plenty of bears. One of the hotspots is Anan Creek, home to one of the largest runs of pink salmon in Southeast Alaska. The run is a very popular dinner spot among black and brown bears.

According to Friends of Anan, 39 bears are known to feast on the salmon at Anan Creek. The population includes 31 black bears and eight brown bears. Like their chunky counterparts farther to the north in Katmai National Park, the bears return each summer to feast on salmon and teach their cubs how to fish in order to survive. And also like the fat bears of Katmai, every bear here has their own habits and quirks. Volverine would fit right in with Katmai’s chunky brown bears, despite the fact that black bears tend to be a bit smaller.

The U.S. Forest Service tracks and documents every bear spotted at Anan, while explore.org’s cameras broadcast live streams of what they are up to 24/7.

First up is a trip to Cray Wanderers on Tuesday 10 November (19:45), followed by a visit to Welling United on Saturday 14 November (15:00), before another Tuesday evening journey to Dartford on 17 November (19:45).

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/digital-health/oura-acquire-ai-virtual-primary-care-partner-counsel-health-625m" hreflang="en">Oura to acquire AI virtual primary care partner Counsel Health for $625M</a>

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →
Oura to acquire AI virtual primary care partner Counsel Health for $625M" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Smart ring maker and health tracking company Oura plans to acquire AI virtual primary care company and business partner Counsel Health for $625 million, the health tech companies announced Tuesday.

The all-cash deal is expected to close before the end of this year, pending customary closing conditions. Bringing Counsel’s platform and capabilities in-house will allow more Oura members to bring their biometric data into a virtual, in-app care encounter, the companies said in their announcement.

“Oura helps members understand their physiology and, with Counsel, they can take action on their health across a range of needs, from quick questions and prescriptions to lab results, chronic care, and referrals,” Tom Hale, CEO of Oura, said. “Together, we can create a new model of care that extends the current healthcare paradigm by delivering an experience that’s more immediate, personalized, and proactive. This is a massive step in our mission to empower people to live healthier, longer, by delivering on the promise of a 24-7 doctor in your pocket.”

Counsel’s model uses text-based, records-informed medical AI encounters to help field and triage users, providing a path to telehealth encounters with the company’s in-house physicians, who are also supported on the backend by a proprietary “Clinician Cockpit” that surfaces relevant clinical data for the encounter. The two-year-old company boasts enterprise partnerships with health plans, provider organizations, employers and other consumer health organizations, for whom it says its approach can reduce costs and improve access to timely care.

Oura is a digital health giant with 5.7 million paid members, whose recently postponed IPO was targeting a $15.6 billion valuation. The company says it is profitable and “growing meaningfully” with 92% year-over-year revenue growth (nearly $1.8 billion) for the fiscal year ended Sept. 30, but ultimately delayed the offering due to “uncertainty” in the market. Its most recent fiscal quarter, ended Sept. 30, included 153% year-over-year revenue growth an adjusted EBITDA of $87 million.

Oura has partnered with Counsel since June, and in September announced a tighter collaboration centered on the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services’ ACCESS (Advancing Chronic Care with Effective Scalable Solutions) Model, set to begin early next year. The companies have said early data from their partnership show strong care satisfaction among those who saw a physician (4.6 out of 5 average rating), widespread agreement among Counsel members (90%) that use of their Oura data helped improve care, and general Oura member satisfaction (70%) with the onboarding to Counsel’s services.

“At Counsel, we’ve built medical AI around trust, safety and responsibility, keeping physicians in the loop for clinical decisions,” Muthu Alagappan, M.D., founder and CEO of Counsel, said in the announcement. “Oura’s data gives clinicians objective context, trended biometrics, and member engagement to deliver more informed care. From the start, our teams bonded over a joint vision, shared culture, and mission, further solidifying as we negotiated our deal over the past few months.”

Under a new owner, the company will continue to serve its existing enterprise partners while expanding to new customers and broadening its clinical capacity. For the latter, Oura and Counsel said they plan to bolster preventive care via continuous monitoring and proactive guidance; acute care via timely intervention for episodic health issues; and specialty and chronic care management in areas like reproductive health, cardiometabolic health and aging.

“Oura tells members what’s happening in their bodies, and Counsel helps them act on it,” summarized Oura Executive Chairman David Shuman. “This combination expands our addressable market and deepens the value we deliver to every member. This not only increases lifetime value, but Counsel’s enterprise partners also open the door to millions of people beyond our existing member base.”

Two Ginger Brothers Became the First Documented Cases of Marfan Syndrome in Cats

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Catster is reader-supported. When you buy via links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission at no cost to you. Learn more.

Cats come in all shapes and sizes. Some have legs that seem a little too long for their bodies, others have ears big enough to pick up satellite signals, and a few look like they haven't quite grown into themselves yet.

But when Gary and Shaggy were kittens, their disproportionately long limbs turned out to be more than an unusual-looking but perfectly charming physical trait. The two brothers would eventually become the first domestic cats confirmed to have Marfan syndrome through genetic testing; not exactly what any cat owner would expect when bringing home a pair of kittens.

As the cats grew, veterinarians identified abnormalities affecting their eyes and enlargement of the aorta, the major blood vessel that carries blood away from the heart. Researchers at Cornell University College of Veterinary Medicine and the Baker Institute for Animal Health worked with veterinary and genetic specialists to investigate what was going on.

Their long limbs were one clue. The eye abnormalities and enlarged aortas added two more. Slowly, the pieces of the puzzle began to fit together, pointing researchers toward Marfan syndrome, a rare inherited disorder far better known in human medicine than in veterinary practice. Genetic testing confirmed the connection, uncovering what made these two brothers so unusual.

Marfan syndrome affects connective tissue, the body's natural support system. This tissue helps give structure and strength to the bones, ligaments, skin, eyes, and blood vessels. When something goes wrong with this system, the effects can show up in several different ways.

In humans, the condition often causes unusually long limbs and fingers, a tall, slender build, eye abnormalities, and potentially serious cardiovascular complications.

Abraham Lincoln was famously tall and lanky, which has led to speculation that he may have had Marfan syndrome. No one can say for sure, though. Turns out, even Honest Abe left a few medical mysteries behind!

For Olympic volleyball player Flo Hyman, long limbs gave her an edge on the court. However, her life was tragically cut short, dying at age 31 from cardiac complications from Marfan syndrome.

One of the genes involved in Marfan syndrome is called FBN1. It contains the instructions for producing fibrillin-1, a protein that helps maintain the structure and function of connective tissues throughout the body.

When that gene doesn't work as it should, the effects can extend well beyond a person's or animal's appearance. As the gorgeous ginger duo, Gary and Shaggy, began showing signs similar to those seen in humans with Marfan syndrome, their veterinary team started to wonder if cats could also suffer from the same condition.

Genetic sequencing revealed that Gary and Shaggy both carried two altered copies of FBN1. In other words, they had inherited the mutation from both parents.

In humans, Marfan syndrome typically develops when a person inherits one disease-causing copy of the gene. Having two altered copies is exceptionally rare and can be associated with more severe disease.

So the brothers presented researchers with an interesting puzzle.

Their particular genetic variant didn't completely prevent the gene from functioning. Instead, it partially disrupted how the gene's instructions were processed, allowing some normal fibrillin-1 function to remain, which may explain why Gary and Shaggy survived into adulthood despite carrying two copies of the variant.

Their genetic instructions weren't simply switched on or off; it was something in between, which may help explain why the brothers developed the way they did.

Genes rarely behave like a tidy set of instructions. The effects of a genetic change depend on the specific variant involved and how it affects the resulting protein. Sometimes, a seemingly insignificant variation can have catastrophic consequences, altering the way the body functions.

The case of Gary and Shaggy is undeniably rare, and Marfan syndrome is unlikely to find its way onto the list of common feline diseases. However, in diagnosing and documenting the condition, veterinarians have something they didn't have before: a confirmed case of feline Marfan syndrome to help recognize, investigate, and compare with future cases.

These findings may help veterinary teams identify similar cases and better understand how particular FBN1 variants affect cats. They also demonstrate the value of bringing clinicians, genetic researchers, and pet parents together when a case doesn't fit neatly into an existing diagnosis.

Gary and Shaggy's shared genetic variant offered researchers a valuable opportunity to investigate the relationship between their physical features and the underlying condition.

Their owner, Nicole Desmond, also contributed to this process, and Cornell has highlighted the case as an example of how pet parents can collaborate with veterinary and genetic experts to advance knowledge that may eventually benefit other animals.

Even more importantly, Nicole loved the unique feline brothers with her whole heart. Vets did not expect either cat to survive for long, but they defied the odds. Gary was 6 years old when he passed away in July 2024, and in November 2025, at the incredible age of 7, Shaggy joined his brother across the rainbow bridge.

And that's one of the interesting things about veterinary medicine. Not every discovery starts with a large clinical trial or a disease affecting thousands of patients. Sometimes, it begins with two unusual kittens and a veterinary team willing to keep asking questions.

The rarity of this case means we donât necessarily have to think the worst when we see a long-legged kitten; itâs far more likely that theyâre simply long-limbed and not harboring a rare genetic disorder. However, it reminds us to pay attention to the bigger picture, bring unusual findings to the veterinarian's attention, and not automatically assume that two seemingly unrelated problems have nothing to do with each other.

Sometimes, a strange-looking kitten is simply a strange-looking kitten.

And sometimes, those extra-long legs are the first clue to a genetic story that researchers have never documented in cats before.

Dr. Paola Cuevas is the Senior In-House Veterinarian at Pangolia, the parent company of Catster. She has over 18 years of experience as a veterinarian and animal behaviorist. Paola's focus is on preventive medicine and animal welfare. Paola has a large range of skills in from diagnostic imaging to nutrition and clinical pathology. Paola enjoys sharing her extensive knowledge of animal training with our Catster readers and is always happy to answer reader questions as our in-house veterinarian.

United Airlines Pilot Couldn’t Fly After A Stroke—His Union Urged Removal, And His Military Bias Suit Failed

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A United Airlines pilot left for military duty in September 2001, planning to come back two years later. Instead, he was furloughed, suffered a stroke, lost his medical clearance and eventually learned that his own union had recommended removing him from the airline’s seniority list. Twenty-five years after that leave began, William Michael Bumpus has lost his military discrimination lawsuit. On November 6, a federal judge in Chicago ruled that he hadn’t produced evidence that his military service motivated United’s decisions. United hired Bumpus in June 2000 and assigned him to Denver. He took approved leave in September 2001 to serve with the Air Force Reserve’s 706th Fighter Squadron, expecting to return in September 2003. Then United furloughed him in 2002, along with many other pilots. The airline struggled after 9/11 and went through a prolonged bankruptcy. According to his amended complaint, he was at Barksdale Air Force Base when a United supervisor called. He explained that he’d stay employed by the Air Force. On September 5, 2006, Bumpus suffered a stroke while on active duty. He was hospitalized and lost the medical certification he needed to fly. The record establishes that this happened during military service, but there’s no indication military service caused the stroke. United recalled him in May 2007. He accepted and told the airline about his medical condition. He didn’t request leave, although the recall offered that option. In January 2008, United told him he’d remain furloughed, with his recall rights expiring in March 2012 if he didn’t obtain medical clearance. He couldn’t fly for United, but the airline was still holding his space open. His employee record showed United returning him to active status and furloughing him again on March 2, 2009. A new furlough could start a fresh ten-year recall period, extending his right to return until March 2019. United offered another recall in 2012, and he requested a military deferment, which the airline acknowledged. His military service ended in March 2013. He still lacked medical clearance and bypassed another recall that September. In 2015, United’s furlough return coordinator told him he’d looked further into the case and confirmed the March 2019 deadline. Then, in 2017, the Air Line Pilots Association reviewed irregularities in United’s seniority list. The union recommended removing Bumpus. United agreed, concluding that his recall rights had expired in 2012. In January 2019, Bumpus sought to return and obtain medical leave to preserve his employment while resolving his medical certification. United initially told him his recall rights ran until March 2019. Days later, the airline reversed itself: its review with the union showed those rights had already expired. He finally obtained a first class medical certificate in June 2020, nearly fourteen years after the stroke. United had offered to consider him as a new hire, but he didn’t apply. He wanted his original seniority. Bumpus pursued a grievance and, in 2021, sued United and the union to obtain access to arbitration. The union wouldn’t take his case forward. He eventually got an arbitration hearing, but his grievance was dismissed as untimely in March 2023. He filed a lawsuit in June 2023, seeking reinstatement with full seniority, lost pay and other damages. He alleged that United accommodated other pilots and brought back 111 pilots without military service while refusing to restore him. His claims under the Uniformed Services Employment and Reemployment Rights Act took two different approaches: United owed him reemployment after military service. This included his argument that it should accommodate his medical condition through leave or an appropriate alternative position. United treated him worse because of his military service. He pointed to other pilots who received leave or returned with their seniority intact. I’ve covered an American Airlines case involving a reservist who couldn’t legally fly but was nevertheless entitled to prompt reemployment in an appropriate role. But Bumpus lost his reemployment claims in May 2025. The judge found that his absence from September 2001 to his accepted recall in May 2007 exceeded the law’s five-year service limit. He argued that time spent on economic furlough shouldn’t count: United wasn’t offering him work during those years anyway. What remained was discrimination. Bumpus needed evidence that his military service was at least one reason for United’s actions. His argument about leave ran into a basic problem: he hadn’t requested it in 2007. He also hadn’t shown that the other pilots received leave without asking. United’s treatment of those pilots didn’t establish that it denied him the same benefit because he served in the military. He did seek medical accommodation in 2019. By then, however, United and the union believed his recall rights had expired. The judge assumed, for purposes of deciding the case, that Bumpus was right about the 2019 deadline. Even with that assumption, he hadn’t shown military bias. United also removed two pilots with no military background after concluding their recall rights had expired, too. So even if United miscalculated, it wasn’t motivated by anti-military bias. The pilot here wasn’t punished for his service. He couldn’t fly for United for years and they held out a spot for him anyway. It’s a complicated case that generated contradictory answers about exactly what his rights were under a union contract and government requirements. The union agreed he should lose his seniority. His medical clearance eventually expired again in 2024. He last flew for United a quarter century ago, and he can’t currently fly even if he wanted to. Topics on this pageAir Line Pilots Association, InternationalUniformed Services Employment and Re-employment Rights Act of 1994United AirlinesAmerican AirlinesChicagoDenver+3 more More From View from the Wing

Twenty-five years after that leave began, William Michael Bumpus has lost his military discrimination lawsuit. On November 6, a federal judge in Chicago ruled that he hadn’t produced evidence that his military service motivated United’s decisions.

United hired Bumpus in June 2000 and assigned him to Denver. He took approved leave in September 2001 to serve with the Air Force Reserve’s 706th Fighter Squadron, expecting to return in September 2003.

Then United furloughed him in 2002, along with many other pilots. The airline struggled after 9/11 and went through a prolonged bankruptcy. According to his amended complaint, he was at Barksdale Air Force Base when a United supervisor called. He explained that he’d stay employed by the Air Force.

On September 5, 2006, Bumpus suffered a stroke while on active duty. He was hospitalized and lost the medical certification he needed to fly. The record establishes that this happened during military service, but there’s no indication military service caused the stroke.

United recalled him in May 2007. He accepted and told the airline about his medical condition. He didn’t request leave, although the recall offered that option. In January 2008, United told him he’d remain furloughed, with his recall rights expiring in March 2012 if he didn’t obtain medical clearance. He couldn’t fly for United, but the airline was still holding his space open.

His employee record showed United returning him to active status and furloughing him again on March 2, 2009. A new furlough could start a fresh ten-year recall period, extending his right to return until March 2019.

United offered another recall in 2012, and he requested a military deferment, which the airline acknowledged. His military service ended in March 2013. He still lacked medical clearance and bypassed another recall that September.

In 2015, United’s furlough return coordinator told him he’d looked further into the case and confirmed the March 2019 deadline.

Then, in 2017, the Air Line Pilots Association reviewed irregularities in United’s seniority list. The union recommended removing Bumpus. United agreed, concluding that his recall rights had expired in 2012.

In January 2019, Bumpus sought to return and obtain medical leave to preserve his employment while resolving his medical certification. United initially told him his recall rights ran until March 2019. Days later, the airline reversed itself: its review with the union showed those rights had already expired.

He finally obtained a first class medical certificate in June 2020, nearly fourteen years after the stroke. United had offered to consider him as a new hire, but he didn’t apply. He wanted his original seniority.

Bumpus pursued a grievance and, in 2021, sued United and the union to obtain access to arbitration. The union wouldn’t take his case forward. He eventually got an arbitration hearing, but his grievance was dismissed as untimely in March 2023.

He filed a lawsuit in June 2023, seeking reinstatement with full seniority, lost pay and other damages. He alleged that United accommodated other pilots and brought back 111 pilots without military service while refusing to restore him. His claims under the Uniformed Services Employment and Reemployment Rights Act took two different approaches:

I’ve covered an American Airlines case involving a reservist who couldn’t legally fly but was nevertheless entitled to prompt reemployment in an appropriate role.

But Bumpus lost his reemployment claims in May 2025. The judge found that his absence from September 2001 to his accepted recall in May 2007 exceeded the law’s five-year service limit. He argued that time spent on economic furlough shouldn’t count: United wasn’t offering him work during those years anyway.

What remained was discrimination. Bumpus needed evidence that his military service was at least one reason for United’s actions. His argument about leave ran into a basic problem: he hadn’t requested it in 2007. He also hadn’t shown that the other pilots received leave without asking. United’s treatment of those pilots didn’t establish that it denied him the same benefit because he served in the military.

He did seek medical accommodation in 2019. By then, however, United and the union believed his recall rights had expired. The judge assumed, for purposes of deciding the case, that Bumpus was right about the 2019 deadline. Even with that assumption, he hadn’t shown military bias. United also removed two pilots with no military background after concluding their recall rights had expired, too. So even if United miscalculated, it wasn’t motivated by anti-military bias.

The pilot here wasn’t punished for his service. He couldn’t fly for United for years and they held out a spot for him anyway. It’s a complicated case that generated contradictory answers about exactly what his rights were under a union contract and government requirements. The union agreed he should lose his seniority. His medical clearance eventually expired again in 2024. He last flew for United a quarter century ago, and he can’t currently fly even if he wanted to.

CoreWeave earnings put debt-fueled AI build-out under scrutiny

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

CoreWeave faces a pivotal test when it reports its third-quarter financial results after the bell on Tuesday.

The market will be closely watching what the neocloud company says about artificial intelligence spending — and how it plans to finance its infrastructure buildout. The outcome could either calm concerns over its debt or stoke more fear.

"Investors need neoclouds to quantify how much financing they expect to raise to execute on existing backlog," Wolfe Research said in a note Monday. "We think outlooks that communicate when and which avenues of funding will be tapped will increase investor confidence."

CoreWeave stock and bonds have come under pressure as fears deepened about the enormous amount of debt it has taken out to build its data centers.

Its stock is down roughly 40% over the past month. Meanwhile, the yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 15.5% on Tuesday, after jumping to 16.7% on Thursday as investors digested the election results and possible roadblocks to data center construction. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

The spread on its five-year credit default swaps — which is insurance against default and considered a barometer of perceived credit risk — is also at levels that are considered distressed. On Tuesday, its CDS spread stood around 1,152 basis points, after rising above its October peak to close at 1,278 basis points on Thursday. Rising CDS spreads mean investors view the debt as riskier.

In addition, the New Jersey-based company's leverage is high. CoreWeave has a debt-to-equity ratio of 702%, up from almost 291% a year ago.

CEO Michael Intrator has sought to quell investor fear, posting on LinkedIn after the election last week that the company doesn't anticipate "any impact to CoreWeave's existing customer commitments or associated backlog as a result of shifts in the political landscape."

The company reported a revenue backlog of $104.2 billion in the second quarter. It has about $35 billion in debt, as of June 30.

CoreWeave has already given a picture of what it expects for the third quarter.

It guided for quarterly revenue of $3.4 billion to $3.6 billion when it reported second-quarter results in August. It anticipated capital expenditures between $11.5 billion and $13.5 billion for the quarter and $35 billion to $39 billion for the full year. The company also expects 1.85 gigawatts in active power by year-end.

Wells Fargo expects the company's capex needs for the rest of the year to be largely covered by its remaining undrawn debt, debt issuances in August/September and the $12 billion it is holding for project costs, which will flow into capex, analyst Michael Turrin said in a note Sunday.

However, its 2027 capex could reach $66 billion, based on an expected capacity addition of 1.5 gigawatts, Turrin said. That will require at least $25 billion more in debt, he noted. It can be largely funded through delayed draw term loans, which allows CoreWeave to draw down funds over time, and the rest can come through bonds and convertibles, he said.

Turrin has an overweight rating and $115 price target on the stock, implying 135% upside from Monday's close.

"While neoclouds sit in the eye of the AI storm, we remain confident that CRWV is the highest quality co in the space," Turrin wrote. "Beyond the noise, we see capacity/revs coming in better than expected (1.95GW this yr vs guide 1.85GW) as margins start to inflect."

However, investors likely won't get all the answers they're seeking during CoreWeave's third-quarter results, said CreditSights analyst Jordan Chalfin.

"One of the major issues for CoreWeave is whether or not they can obtain all the financing they need," he said. "They may try and calm the markets, but I'm not sure how much color they can and will provide."

Investors will be looking for 2027 guidance on capex and free cash flow, though that may not come until Coreweave releases fourth quarter results, Chalfin notes.

CoreWeave could ease investor concerns by showing it has plans and approvals in place to scale capacity through 2027, he said. The company can also demonstrate remaining performance obligation (RPO) growth, which shows the total value of its contracted future revenue, and highlight new customer wins.

In addition, pricing has been really strong on recent deals, Chalfin pointed out. "That could show up in an operating margin beat for Q3 and expectations for Q4."

While CoreWeave faces finances challenges, the company has many ways to access capital, Chalfin said. That includes chip-backed deals, which allow Corewave to borrow money using GPUs as collateral, and original equipment manufacturer financing. For instance, CoreWeave could turn to Dell, which is a big supplier with a strong balance sheet, Chalfin noted.

"Despite all the market sentiment, fundamentals are actually quite strong and pricing is strong," he said. "The trends over the next few quarters, I think, should be supportive." Risks could arise after that, but it is "too early to call."

Some analysts remain skeptical that Coreweave will be able to calm credit fears.

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, points out that the company is facing a huge bill in just over two years. CoreWeave owes roughly $15 billion in principal payments through 2028, according to its second-quarter earnings statement.

"They don't have enough cash to service all that debt," he said. "Unless some big orders come in, unless some big money flows come in for them in the next 17, 18 months, I think this is going to be the start of the end for CoreWeave, unless somebody comes in and wants to buy them. But who wants to buy all that debt?"

However, there could be a "small" window for the company to push out its debt for another three to five years, but it will depend on how strong their cash flow is when they report on Tuesday, Lang said.

Plus, issuing equity to help fund infrastructure projects, like Meta and Oracle did recently, isn't an option for CoreWeave, he noted.

"They're going to dilute the stock so much that it's going to continue to go down," he said. "They missed that window when the stock was in the hundreds."

CoreWeave topped $183 in June 2025. It has since declined more than 73%, recently trading around $47.

Despite the fall, analysts remain bullish on the stock with an average overweight rating and $126 target, based on 39 estimates complied by FactSet.

—CNBC's Jordan Novet contributed reporting.

Rockstar details Grand Theft Auto 6 PS5 Pro enhancements, but it'll still only run at 30FPS

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Pushing consoles to "their absolute limits".

As Grand Theft Auto 6 preloads go live, developer Rockstar has detailed the enhancements coming to the PS5 Pro version of the game, confirming these won't include framerates above the 30 FPS its targeting for consoles.

Rockstar shared the news in a blog post released to announce that GTA 6 pre-loading is now available ahead of its release date on 19th November.

"Grand Theft Auto 6 has been fully developed from the ground up to provide the best possible experience for the current generation of consoles," Rockstar says. It goes on to reconfirm GTA 6 is targeting 30 FPS on all consoles, saying the game is pushing modern machines "to their absolute limits" in order to "bring the vast and rich world of Leonida to life — while maintaining the density and visual fidelity of all its environments and inhabitants."

PS5 players can expect high-fidelity haptic feedback through the DualSense controllers, improved spatial audio, and Tempest 3D audio, while PS5 Pro enhancements come in the form of PSSR support and ray tracing shadows, which should make the game look especially fancy, plus improved load times. PlayStation players can also expect remote play support. As for the Xbox, the post clarified details on Xbox Cloud Gaming, which will allow the game to be streamed to Xbox consoles including the Xbox One. Notably, this won't work on PC.

Grand Theft Auto 6's 30 FPS target was previously confirmed by Rockstar back in August, but there was some uncertainty around a possible 60fps performance mode at the time. Still, those PS5 technical boons do a lot of work to improve the games it's applied to, which if nothing else should mean GTA 6 will be quite the looker.
